input
file_path /Users/jalex/projects/clawdpop/README.md
result
1	# clawdpop
2	
3	Suno + Claude Code music videos in the "I'm Upping My P(doom)" lineage (Suno "Claude-Pop"
4	song → forced-aligned lyrics → every frame a deterministic function of song time, painted
5	in a browser and exported through headless Chrome and ffmpeg).
6	
7	## Songs
8	
9	- `songs/2am-crying/` — "2AM (And I'm Crying)": untz-untz Eurodance from the point of view of
10	  a one-year-old. `LYRICS.md`, `TREATMENT.md`, `SUNO_RUNS.md`, `synth/` (procedural demo of
11	  the backing track), `video/` (renderer scenes).
12	- `songs/baby-facts/` — dense fact-rap about infant science (Netflix *Babies* and the
13	  literature behind it). `CONCEPT.md`, `FACTS.md`.
14	
15	## Shared tooling
16	
17	- `shared/renderer/` — song-agnostic engine: timeline, karaoke band, HUD, headless-Chrome
18	  frame export, ffmpeg encode.
19	- `shared/align/` — audio analysis: Demucs vocal stem → CTC forced alignment (torchaudio
20	  MMS_FA) cross-checked with mlx-whisper → `lyrics.json`; librosa beats/downbeats/sections →
21	  `audio.json`.
22	
23	## Pipeline
24	
25	1. Lyrics first. Dense, every line carrying a reference; a fixed hook that escalates.
26	2. Scope Suno runs: custom mode, lyrics with section markers, 2 to 3 style-tag variants.
27	   Driven with the `suno` CLI (`~/.local/bin/suno`, unofficial, costs ~70 credits per v5.5
28	   call). Auth is a manual one-time `suno auth --login`.
29	3. Reification with Opus 5.5 agents: procedural synth demo of the track (so the renderer can
30	   be built before Suno audio exists), renderer engine + plates, alignment pipeline.
31	4. Align the keeper Suno clip, swap the mock timing for real timing, render 1080p60.
32	
33	Audio and video outputs are gitignored; code, specs, JSON timing and contact sheets are
34	committed.
35

1	# 2AM (And I'm Crying): the video
2	
3	Treatment v3, "the baby's selfie video" (`../TREATMENT.md`), over the v3 lyric sheet
4	(`../suno_lyrics_v3_day.txt`). Every frame is a pure function of song time, painted on a
5	canvas in headless Chrome and encoded with ffmpeg. The engine is song-agnostic and lives in
6	`shared/renderer/`; this directory holds the song: clip types, the baby (baby Clawd), props,
7	the caption and status-bar styling, the time-of-day grade and the edit rules.
8	
9	Review surface: `sheets/plates.png` (one cell per clip, 9:16) and `sheets/layout-16x9.png`.
10	
11	## Commands
12	
13	All from the repo root (`/Users/jalex/projects/clawdpop`). One-time setup:
14	
15	```sh
16	npm install --prefix shared/renderer          # puppeteer-core only; drives the installed Chrome
17	```
18	
19	Preview (scrub, play against the audio; space, arrows = beat, shift+arrows = bar, `[` `]` = clip):
20	
21	```sh
22	node shared/renderer/scripts/serve.mjs        # then open:
23	# http://localhost:8123/songs/2am-crying/video/index.html?t=23
24	# http://localhost:8123/songs/2am-crying/video/index.html?layout=16x9&t=40
25	```
26	
27	Draft render over the Suno vocal (9:16, 540x960, 30 fps; about 7 min with the default 9 workers on this Mac):
28	
29	```sh
30	node shared/renderer/scripts/render.mjs --song songs/2am-crying/video \
31	  --audio songs/2am-crying/audio/runF2/it-s-2am-and-i-m-crying-tender-bridge-b-238033cd.mp3 \
32	  --out songs/2am-crying/video/renders/draft-suno-E2-clawd-540p.mp4 --check
33	# 16:9 draft: add --layout 16x9 (960x540) and a different --out
34	# mock timing over the synth demo: --data data-mock (and no --audio)
35	```
36	
37	Final renders (1080p60; `A=songs/2am-crying/audio/runF2/it-s-2am-and-i-m-crying-tender-bridge-b-238033cd.mp3`):
38	
39	```sh
40	node shared/renderer/scripts/render.mjs --song songs/2am-crying/video --audio $A --w 1080 --h 1920 --fps 60 \
41	  --out songs/2am-crying/video/renders/final-1080x1920-60.mp4
42	node shared/renderer/scripts/render.mjs --song songs/2am-crying/video --audio $A --layout 16x9 --w 1920 --h 1080 --fps 60 \
43	  --out songs/2am-crying/video/renders/final-1920x1080-60.mp4
44	```
45	
46	Contact sheets and stills:
47	
48	```sh
49	node shared/renderer/scripts/render.mjs --song songs/2am-crying/video --sheet songs/2am-crying/video/sheets/plates.png
50	node shared/renderer/scripts/render.mjs --song songs/2am-crying/video --layout 16x9 \
51	  --sheet songs/2am-crying/video/sheets/layout-16x9.png --sheet-mode even --count 12 --cols 4 --cell-w 480
52	node shared/renderer/scripts/render.mjs --song songs/2am-crying/video --stills 4.5,40,77
53	node shared/renderer/scripts/smoke.mjs --q t=23 --q "layout=16x9&t=40"   # preview page loads cleanly
54	```
55	
56	Timing and placeholder audio:
57	
58	```sh
59	node songs/2am-crying/video/tools/mock_timing.mjs --out songs/2am-crying/video/data-mock   # mock pair (data/ holds the Suno take)
60	node songs/2am-crying/video/tools/mock_timing.mjs --plan --out songs/2am-crying/video/data-mock   # ignore the synth, use the 128 BPM plan
61	node songs/2am-crying/video/tools/make_click.mjs      # audio/click.wav (fallback audio, gitignored)
62	```
63	
64	`render.mjs` options: `--from/--to` seconds, `--workers N` (default ~60% of cores),
65	`--crf 16`, `--preset medium`, `--audio file`, `--data dir` (timing dir relative to the song dir,
66	default `data/`), `--force` (allow audio/timing length mismatch), `--check` (determinism: ordered vs
67	shuffled frames across workers; loop: outro frames equal intro frames; exit 2 on a failure). Video is libx264 CRF 16, yuv420p, tagged BT.709; audio AAC 192k.
68	
69	## Data
70	
71	Current timing: the Suno take **runF2 238033cd** (`../audio/runF2/it-s-2am-and-i-m-crying-tender-bridge-b-238033cd.mp3`,
72	208.36 s, 131.5 BPM; its v4 tender-bridge lyric sheet is `../suno_lyrics_v4_tender.txt`), aligned by `shared/align` (`align.py` + `analyze.py`, output in
73	`../audio/runF2/data/`, copied here). The mock timing it replaced (synth demo / 128 BPM plan) is in
74	`data-mock/` (`?data=data-mock/` in the preview, `--data data-mock` in render.mjs).
75	
76	- `data/lyrics.json`: `{ lines: [{ i, text, start, end, section, words: [{ w, start, end, conf, syl?, bv? }] }], extras, notes }`
77	  (mexicat/pdoom-video schema). **`section` is load-bearing**: the sheet header's id, `intro, chorus1, verse1,
78	  chorus2, verse2, bridge, final, outro` (the aligner writes it; naming in `shared/align/README.md`). Clip
79	  selection keys on it: verse clauses cut on the half bar, every `bridge` clause goes into the one long take,
80	  every `final` clause into the grid, and the `outro` "Hi" line is the loop.
81	- `data/audio.json`: `{ bpm, beats, downbeats, sections: [{ name, start, end }], kicks, duration }` plus
82	  optional fields: `beats_per_bar`, `hats` (offbeat onsets), `quiet` (silent spans), `risers`, `vocal_onsets`,
83	  `loudness`, `source` (`"synth (...)"`, `"plan"`, or a take with its file in parentheses; the preview
84	  plays that file, render.mjs finds the synth wav itself and otherwise needs `--audio`), `lyrics_sheet` (the sheet, relative to the song root `songs/2am-crying/`
85	  or to the repo root). analyze.py's `sections` are detected structure named `s1..s11`; nothing selects
86	  clips by them.
87	
88	Section precedence in the engine (`shared/renderer/core/lyrics.js`): a line's own `section`, else the
89	section derived from the sheet named in `audio.json.lyrics_sheet` (same naming, matched by line text in
90	order; `[lyrics] ... derived from the lyric sheet` warning), else audio.json's sections (warning; with
91	`s1..s11` no section plate can match). render.mjs prints at start-up where the sections came from and
92	which section plates exist, and says `MISSING` for an absent one:
93	
94	```
95	[render] line sections from lyrics.json: intro, chorus1, verse1, chorus2, verse2, bridge, final, outro
96	[render] section plate ok: bridge long take -> nap:long 2:06.13-2:24.09 (18.0s)
97	[render] section plate ok: final chorus grid -> grid 2:24.09-3:08.98 (44.9s)
98	[render] section plate ok: outro loop -> hello 3:08.98-3:15.56 (6.6s)
99	```
100	
101	`--check` fails (exit 2) when the outro loop is missing or an outro frame differs from its intro frame.
102	The loop is anchored on the "Hi" line: outro frame k shows intro frame k - shift, the shift being the
103	whole-frame distance between the two "Hi"s (about 195.4 s on runF2 238033cd; the outro window starts 0.18 s before its
104	"Hi", the intro 2.80 s before), so the caption stays on the vocal.
105	
106	`tools/mock_timing.mjs` builds the mock pair (`--plan` ignores the synth); write it to `data-mock/`. Clips
107	are derived from lyric content, so a new take only needs its `lyrics.json`/`audio.json` in `data/`. Watch the
108	render log for `[clips] ... matched no rule` (a clause whose text drifted falls back to the closeup clip) and
109	`[timeline] ... moved off a shared downbeat` (two clauses snapped to the same downbeat; the later one moves to
110	the next beat).
111	
112	Audio: for the Suno take pass it explicitly (`--audio songs/2am-crying/audio/runF2/it-s-2am-and-i-m-crying-tender-bridge-b-238033cd.mp3`;
113	the preview finds it from `source`). With the mock data the draft uses `../synth/out/2am_crying.wav`
114	when `audio.json.source` is the synth (the full-song file, chosen by duration); otherwise
115	`audio/click.wav`, a click + kick + hats + riser track generated from audio.json by
116	`tools/make_click.mjs`. render.mjs refuses audio whose length differs from audio.json by more than a bar.
117	
118	## The character: baby Clawd
119	
120	The lead is a baby Clawd, the Claude Code mascot (the protagonist of the "I'm Upping My P(doom)"
121	video), drawn fresh in Canvas 2D in the flat style. Adult Clawd is a 10 x 6 unit clay block on
122	four legs with two arm nubs and tall slit eyes; the baby is the same idea made younger:
123	
124	- body: chubbier and nearly square (2.5r wide, 2.6r tall, r = the scene's `r`), rounder corners,
125	  the sides bowing out a little; terracotta clay `#d77757` with a light patch up and left, dark
126	  ink outline; a single curl on top; four short stubby legs in a darker clay.
127	- face (around `(o.x, o.y)`, where the old round head's centre was, so props still land): eyes
128	  are bigger, lower, rounded rectangles (0.31r x 0.46r at +-0.44r) with a big highlight dot;
129	  small mouth at +0.47r; blush always a little, more on demand (hatched above 0.6).
130	- the phone nub always stretches from the body's side to the bottom corner, widening toward the
131	  lens (the phone itself is never seen). The free nub: `down`, `wave`, `up`, `hold` (to a point
132	  the scene gives, where the scene draws the prop), `point`, `none`.
133	- outfits: `sack` (night: a soft bag over the lower body and the legs, zipped up the middle),
134	  `onesie` (day: a cloth band across the lower body with two snaps), `naked` (bare clay with
135	  Clawd's darker band, after the bath), `towel` (a cape behind).
136	- expressions on the same API as before: eyes `open` / `half` / continuous `open` (a heavy lid
137	  that droops toward the outer corner; with `lidSoft` the eye shortens from the top instead, no
138	  lid line, which reads drowsy and content rather than unimpressed: the bridge) / `closed` (a soft arc) / `squeeze` (> <) / `wide` / `focus`
139	  (a narrow slit); mouth `line` / `o` (the crying O, with a tongue when wide open) / `smile` /
140	  `grin` / `press` (a flat crimped line, implies a furrowed brow) / `none`; `tears` (thick streams
141	  and flicking drops, the value is the animation phase), `blush`, `flash`, `stain` (milk on the
142	  onesie and a dribble), `look` (eyes and highlights shift), `brows`, and the new `pacifier`
143	  (0..1: a shield and ring over the mouth; on for 5am quiet, 7:45 "sleeping", 8pm-12am sleeping,
144	  the 11am and 3pm naps).
145	- `BABY_GEOM` exports the geometry (the 5am / 1am glowing eyes in the dark use it).
146	- the parent is an adult Clawd (`props.adultClawd`): a backlit silhouette down the lit hall at
147	  3am, slumped by the crib at 4am (dim clay, tired eyes over the baby's shoulder), asleep in the
148	  armchair on the rear camera at 10pm (eyes shut, mouth open, z's); the 12pm spoon comes in on an
149	  adult nub; the 7:45 IR monitor cut-in shows baby Clawd in its sack; in the bridge it holds the
150	  baby (a close-up: `adultClawd` with `h` for a body taller than the frame, `arms: null`, `shine`
151	  highlights and `look`, `shoulder` for a rounded silhouette; the nub behind the back is `props.hand`,
152	  the one the baby sits in a curved round-ended nub), and leans in on "I love you".
153	
154	Palette keys (`tod.js` `BASE`, re-lit by `grade()`, and the night `FLASH` / intro `SCREEN` sets):
155	`skin` (clay), `skinShade`, `skinLight`, `line`, `ink`, `mouth`, `blush`, `tear`, `paci`,
156	`paciRing`, `parentSkin`, `parentShade`.
157	
158	## How it is put together
159	
160	- `clips.js`: splits every lyric line into time clauses ("It's <time> ..."), maps each clause's
161	  text to a clip type + params (the rule table), and returns the timeline: one window per
162	  clause (cut on the downbeat; on the half-bar in the double-time verses, so two cuts per bar),
163	  one long-take window for the bridge, one grid window for the final chorus, and the outro.
164	- `story.js`: status-bar clock (the clause's time; minute by minute in the long take), battery
165	  (drains, charges with a bolt during naps, ends the day in the red), record timer, and the
166	  loop: outro frames are intro frames, quantized to the frame grid (`--check` verifies it).
167	- `song.js`: the compositor. Clip under the camera transform (handshake + a slight kick shake +
168	  per-clip tilt, owned here so a clip can be held still) -> lens post (grain on a fixed 24 Hz
169	  tick, vignette) -> lens overlays (splat, droplets, finger) -> caption -> status bar; then the
170	  layout: full frame (9:16) or the phone centred on a soft nursery backdrop with a sun/moon
171	  arc (16:9).
172	- `tod.js`: time-of-day light (deep blue night, grey-blue 5am, warm 7am, white noon, gold 6pm,
173	  blue 8pm), the night flash palette, the phone-screen glow.
174	- `baby.js` (baby Clawd), `props.js` (rooms, props, the adult Clawd parent), `monitor.js` (the v1
175	  IR monitor view, cut into 7:45 "sleeping").
176	- `scenes/`: one module per clip type: `hello`, `crib`, `nap`, `pooping`, `closeup`, `highchair`,
177	  `standing`, `fan`, `bath`, `rear`, `grid`.
178

212	## Deliberate departures
213	
214	- The baby is baby Clawd, not the treatment's round-headed baby ("the baby is... not that cute?
215	  have we considered baby clawd?"). Its arms are Clawd's nubs, so the phone arm is a stretched
216	  nub rather than an upper arm and elbow; the parent is an adult Clawd.
217	
218	- Verse 2 is 9 bars (the brief's 8 plus the bar of silence after "1am and I'm..."), 57 bars total.
219	- No wide-angle edge stretch: it needs a per-frame offscreen remap for an effect that is barely
220	  visible at phone size. Day "over-saturation" is carried by the time-of-day grade instead.
221	- The 12th grid tile (11 final-chorus lines in a 3x4 grid) is the record button rather than a clip.
222	- The drinking clips hold the bottle with one hand: the other hand is always on the phone.

1	# Treatment: Replicate (n = 1)
2	
3	A music video typeset like the methods section of a paper. Every lyric line is a figure.
4	The baby is the only thing on the page that is in color, and in every figure the baby is
5	the data point. That is the whole visual thesis: "I'm the subject. I'm the sample. I'm the
6	n of one."
7	
8	Primary layout 16:9 (1920x1080). A paper figure is landscape. A 9:16 cut can come later
9	by stacking the figure over the caption.
10	
11	## The page
12	
13	- **Paper.** Cream lab-notebook stock (`#f4efe3`) with a faint 5 mm grid (`#e6dfcf`), a
14	  subtle paper grain (deterministic noise), slightly darker toward the edges. The page
15	  never changes; figures are drawn onto it and erased from it.
16	- **Ink.** One near-black (`#1d1a17`) for everything drawn: axes, apparatus, adults,
17	  arrows, annotations. Strokes have a slight hand wobble (deterministic noise seeded by
18	  the plate and the stroke index, never by wall time), as if inked with a technical pen.
19	  Hatching rather than fills for shade. A second, lighter ink (`#8a8378`) for grid-lines
20	  inside figures, error bars, dotted leaders.
21	- **Color.** Only the baby: baby Clawd from the 2am video (`songs/2am-crying/video/baby.js`,
22	  the clay orange `#D77757` family), now wearing a white EEG cap with ink electrodes and a
23	  ribbon of wires trailing off-frame ("the one in the little cap"). The cap is an opt-in
24	  option; the 2am video must stay pixel-identical. Where a figure has many data points,
25	  the other points are ink; the baby is the orange one. Where the lyric says "you", the
26	  adults (scientists, parents) are ink line drawings of adult Clawd, never colored.
27	- **Type.** Figure captions in a serif (system "Iowan Old Style", fallback Georgia),
28	  italic for the caption label: *Fig. 3.* Numbers and the HUD in a monospace ("SF Mono",
29	  fallback Menlo). The lyric itself is set in the serif as the figure legend, see below.
30	
31	## Three permanent elements
32	
33	1. **The HUD, top-left:** `n = 1` in mono, small, with a thin ink box. It holds `n = 1`
34	   for the whole song except the bridge, where it shows the real sample size of each myth
35	   as that myth is named (`n = 4`, `n = 36 (students)`, `n = 0 words`, `n = 567 · 37 labs`),
36	   then on the drop rolls through digits and settles back to `n = 1`, which it keeps to
37	   the end. The final frame of the song is just the HUD on paper.
38	2. **The figure caption, bottom:** a rule, then `Fig. k.` in italic serif plus a short
39	   title and the citation in lighter ink, e.g. *Fig. 7. Growth saltation in one infant
40	   (Lampl, 1992).* It changes with every figure. The figure numbers run in order across
41	   the song, which is a quiet joke by the final chorus (Fig. 31).
42	3. **The lyric legend:** the current lyric line set in the serif under the caption rule,
43	   two lines maximum, left-aligned, in lighter ink; each word turns full ink as it is sung
44	   (word-synced from `lyrics.json`). Dense lines must be readable: minimum 34 px at 1080p,
45	   wrap at the clause. Parentheticals in the lyric (backing vocals) are set in italic and
46	   smaller. This replaces the lowercase pop captions of the 2am video entirely.
47	
48	## Figures, in song order
49	
50	Each lyric line maps to a figure by text rule (as `clips.js` does in the 2am song). Lines
51	that share a lab share one figure that keeps developing (the slope keeps rising, the
52	scatter keeps filling). Hero moments land on the sung word.
53	
54	**Intro.** The paper title block: `Replicate (n = 1)` as a paper title, then the author
55	line "Fifteen babies · thirty-six world-renowned scientists · one year", then an ECG/fetal
56	heartbeat trace drawing itself along the bottom in time with the heartbeat in the audio.
57	On "And me": the baby in the cap pokes in from the bottom-right corner and waves one nub.
58	This same page is the outro, so the song can loop (see Loop).
59	
60	**Verse 1 (Love).**
61	- *Oxytocin.* Two curves rising on one chart, labeled "mothers" and "fathers", drawn in
62	  step; on "identical in dads" the second curve snaps exactly onto the first. A cotton swab
63	  icon at the axis origin. Caption cites Gordon et al. 2010.
64	- *Still face.* A three-panel strip like a figure with panels a, b, c. (a) adult Clawd in
65	  ink leaning toward the baby; (b) on "froze a mother's face" the adult's face goes flat
66	  and a 2:00 timer starts under the panel; the baby cycles through the bids as they are
67	  sung: smile, point ("bid"), fuss, cry (tears are the only non-orange color in the baby,
68	  pale blue as in the 2am video), hands to mouth; (c) on "Then she comes back" the adult
69	  leans back in and the baby's face settles. "We repair" is the caption title.
70	
71	**Chorus (every time).** The apparatus line: a long bench drawn in ink with the stations
72	named in the lyric left to right, scanner ring, slope, EEG cap on a stand, swab, a ruler
73	marked 7 and 10 (months). The baby rides a small cart along the bench, passing through each
74	station on its word ("scanner" the ring, "slope" the ramp, "cap" the cap drops on, "swab"
75	a swab taps the cheek, "seven, then at ten" the ruler ticks). On "Will it replicate?" the
76	baby splits like a cell: one becomes two, two become four, each copy an ink outline, in a
77	dividing-cell diagram, with the original staying orange. The chorus ends with a 4x4 grid of
78	ink babies and one orange. In the **final chorus**, on each "(I did.)" a row of the grid
79	turns orange, and by "Ask me in a year" all sixteen are orange. That is the payoff; nothing
80	else in the song is colored, so it should feel like the page lighting up.
81	
82	**Verse 2 (Crawling).**
83	- *Barbu-Roth.* The baby in a harness suspended over a floor of streaming dots (optic flow
84	  moving toward the camera, perspective grid), legs swimming on "I swim"; a small inset
85	  of a tumbling astronaut drawn in ink on "astronaut". On "skateboard" the harness lifts
86	  away and the baby is belly-down on a skateboard, crossing a mattress drawn in plan view;
87	  tally marks appear on "Twice. Three times."; on "a rest" the baby stops and the eyes
88	  close for a beat.
89	- *Lampl.* A growth chart, length vs days, with the classic smooth curve in light ink and
90	  the real data as a staircase: flat, flat, flat, a step. The notebook row
91	  `59.8 / 59.8 / 59.8 / 60.8` typed in mono above the step. On "two and a half centimeters
92	  overnight" a step rises; "sixty-three days of flat" draws a long flat run with a
93	  bracket. If the take contains the "front page" line, a newspaper masthead stamps in and
94	  a speech bubble from an ink figure is struck through. The baby stands on the current
95	  step of the staircase, growing by a few pixels with each step.
96	- *Adolph.* The adjustable slope: a ramp on a hinge with a protractor arc, the angle
97	  readout in mono. Each "six degrees" raises it 6° with a ratchet; by "sheer drop" it is
98	  near vertical. The baby is at the top, peering over. If the Grant line is present, an ink
99	  toddler approaches at the shallow pace and goes straight off the edge in a little arc
100	  (no impact drawn, just a dotted trajectory and a "*" footnote). Step counter and fall
101	  counter in the corner tick up to `2,368 /h` and `17`. On "Crawling isn't mandatory" a row
102	  of small ink icons for bum-shuffle, bear-crawl, crawl, with the baby doing the
103	  bum-shuffle, happily.
104	
105	**Verse 3 (Words, Sleep).**
106	- *Hirsh-Pasek* (if present). A speech waveform of a Cinderella sentence, drawn as an ink
107	  envelope, with pauses inserted: ones at clause boundaries marked with a tick, one in the
108	  middle of a clause marked with a cross. Two loudspeaker icons left and right; the baby's
109	  head turns to the natural one.
110	- *Saffran.* A stream of syllables `pa bee coo go la too ...` scrolling by in mono, with
111	  arcs above showing transitional probabilities: `1.0` arcs inside words, `0.33` between.
112	  A counter of repetitions ticks to 45 over two minutes on the HUD line. On "I'm carving
113	  speech" the word boundaries cut in as vertical lines and the words stack.
114	- *Jarvis.* A phylogenetic tree in ink. On "three birds, five mammals" the vocal-learner
115	  branches thicken and get their labels in order as they are sung: parrots, bats, elephants,
116	  seals, dolphins, songbirds, hummingbirds; on "This girl" the last branch ends in the baby,
117	  orange, holding a tiny microphone. A small dancing silhouette footnote for "danced six
118	  hours a day".
119	- *Fenton.* A scatter: x is time of day (0 to 24 h), y is age in months (0 to 12). Sleep
120	  events as dots. At one month the dots are noise; as "a hint by eight" and "a rhythm by a
121	  year" are sung, bands form (night sleep, two naps). Big counters in mono roll: 1,000
122	  babies, 6,000,000 events. On "(He hasn't published it.)" a rubber stamp hits the figure:
123	  `UNPUBLISHED`, at a slight angle. The caption reads "(Fenton & Wallisch, n.d.)".
124	- *Blumberg.* The baby asleep on its side in the cap. On "twitch" one leg jerks and a sonar
125	  ring expands from it; a body map beside it (a schematic figure, ink) lights the matching
126	  limb; "a thousand neurons" shows as a burst of dots along the map.
127	- *Spencer* (if present). Two small boxes labeled hippocampus and cortex; sleep spindles
128	  drawn as waveform bursts carry index cards from one box to the other. Two naps and a
129	  night as three cards.
130	
131	**Verse 4 (First Steps, Part Two).**
132	- *Dominici* (if present). Two EMG traces, then four; a row of ink animals walking:
133	  rat, cat, macaque, guineafowl, then the baby.
134	- *Cowgill.* A suitcase labeled `130 lb` dragged on a dotted path across a map of fifteen
135	  tiny museum icons (columns and a pediment), a counter to 600 at the side. On "Reviewer 2"
136	  a margin note in a different hand appears: "children's bones don't respond to exercise",
137	  and on "Take that back" a femur cross-section morphs from a wide oval to a teardrop and
138	  the note is struck through.
139	- *Walle* (if present). A word counter like a lab LENA device; two babies, one crawling,
140	  one walking, identical word count heard, different word count known.
141	- *Hespos.* A block hanging in midair with no support, a question mark drawn and erased;
142	  looking-time bars: the "possible" bar short, the "impossible" bar twice as long, growing
143	  as the baby stares.
144	- *Kang Lee.* Two monkey faces drawn in ink at six months, clearly different; on "Nine
145	  months" they blur toward one another until identical; the adult Clawd in the margin
146	  shrugs ("like you, not at all").
147	- *Hamlin.* A hill in profile; a round puppet climbs, a square puppet shoves it down, a
148	  triangle pushes it up; the baby's nub reaches for the helper. Caption cites Hamlin et al.
149	  2007 and adds "(but see Fig. 27)", pointing at the bridge.
150	- *Warneken.* An ink adult "drops" a cup, dotted fall; a stopwatch counts to 10 s as the
151	  baby picks it up and hands it back. On "Pay me with a toy" a toy appears; on "I'll stop"
152	  the baby sits down and folds its nubs. "Also the human part" is the caption title.
153	
154	**Bridge (the part you skip).** The page turns dim (ink wash at 15 percent), the figures are
155	journal covers and products drawn as ink line art, one per myth, each stamped. The HUD takes
156	the sample size as each lands:
157	- The Wonder Weeks: a calendar with ten leaps circled; stamp `n = 4`; a second stamp
158	  `1998` over a small figure leaving a door.
159	- Mozart effect: a CD; stamp `n = 36 (college students)`; a stack of CDs with a Georgia
160	  peach outline; the HUD shows `$105,000` for a beat.
161	- Baby Einstein: a DVD case; stamp `0 new words`; a receipt prints out: `REFUND $15.99`.
162	- Neonatal imitation (if present): a tongue-out photo as an ink sketch; `n = 6 (1977)` and
163	  under it `n = 106 (2016): nothing`.
164	- The helper and the hinderer: the puppet hill from Fig. 20 returns, small, under a
165	  journal masthead `Nature, 2007`; on "ManyBabies four" the figure multiplies into
166	  thirty-seven tiny hills; the number `49.3 %` and a coin, drawn, flipping in the air.
167	- Thirty million words (if present): a counter at 30,000,000 with `n = 42 · 1 h / month`
168	  stamped across it.
169	- "So when you ask me... will it replicate?": everything on the page fades to lighter ink
170	  except the HUD, which shows the myth sample sizes in a fast shuffle.
171	
172	**Break ("Here's what did.").** The page goes blank. Only the paper, the caption rule, the
173	HUD at `n = ?`. One beat of nothing.
174	
175	**Drop.** A single ink cell in the center. It divides on the beat: 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32, the
176	outlines packing into the shape of a brain in profile; a volume counter in mono rolls to
177	`+101 %`; `2,500 synapses / neuron` ticks up and then is visibly pruned (lines erased);
178	`weight x3` and `length x1.5` appear as a small table. The baby appears in the center of the
179	brain, orange, on "Me." On "I'm the replication" the HUD settles on `n = 1`.
180	
181	**Final chorus.** The apparatus bench again, but the grid payoff described above: the
182	copies turn orange row by row on "(I did.)".
183	
184	**Outro.** Back to the title page. The heartbeat trace continues from where the intro left
185	it. "Hardest problem in the universe. We should let them sleep." is set as a pull quote in
186	the serif, attributed in lighter ink. On "n equals one" the title block fades and leaves the
187	HUD alone on the paper: `n = 1`. The baby is asleep in the corner in the cap.
188	
189	## Loop
190	
191	Like the 2am video: the outro frame is the intro frame, so the file loops. The loop anchor is
192	the heartbeat trace and the title block, which are identical at the start and the end; the
193	`--check` loop test in `render.mjs` must pass.
194	
195	## Motion rules
196	
197	- Everything is drawn in time: strokes grow, numbers roll, curves plot left to right.
198	  Nothing pops in except rubber stamps, which hit on the beat with a small squash.
199	- Beat-synced motion is allowed in the choruses (the cart, the cell division) and the drop.
200	  In verses the motion follows the words, not the beat; this is a documentary, not a club.
201	- The baby's expression follows the lyric: curious in the labs, unimpressed in the bridge,
202	  asleep in the outro. Reuse the 2am baby's expression parameters.
203	- Deterministic: every frame is a pure function of song time, as in the 2am renderer.
204	
205	## Data
206	
207	- Sheet: `songs/baby-facts/suno_lyrics_v1_short.txt` (the keeper's sheet). Sections:
208	  intro, verse1, chorus1, verse2, verse3, chorus2, verse4, bridge, break, drop, final, outro.
209	- Keeper for development: `songs/baby-facts/audio/runA/replicate-n-1-6b228558.mp3`
210	  (4:29, 95.6 BPM), aligned in `songs/baby-facts/audio/runA/data/`.
211	- A radio cut (`suno_lyrics_v2_radio.txt`, seven lines shorter) is being generated in runs
212	  C and C2; if a take is good it replaces the keeper, so figures keyed to lines that the
213	  radio cut dropped (Grant, Hirsh-Pasek, the "ninety-five percent" line, Fenton's event
214	  counts, neonatal imitation, thirty million words) must degrade gracefully when the line
215	  is absent.

=== engine.js
// Engine: wires audio.json + lyrics.json + the song's timeline into a pure render(t).
//
// A song module (see songs/<song>/video/song.js) provides:
//   design   { w, h }                 design coordinate space (1920x1080), or
//   layouts  { name: { w, h } }        several (e.g. '9x16', '16x9'); defaultLayout picks one
//   fonts    [{ family, weight, style? }]
//   timeline [spec entries]            see timeline.js
//   scenes   { plateId: { draw(ctx, S) } }
//   setup(env)                         optional; derive song-level curves (story clock...)
//   frame(ctx, t, env)                 composites one frame (scene, post, HUD, karaoke)
//   sheetCells(env)                    optional; extra contact-sheet cells [{ t, label }]
//
// Frames carry no state between calls: render(t) for any t, in any order, on any worker.

import { makeBeats } from './beats.js';
import { makeLyrics } from './lyrics.js';
import { buildTimeline } from './timeline.js';
import { clamp } from './util.js';

export function createEngine({ song, lyricsJson, audioJson, width, height, fps = 30, layout = null, sheet = null }) {
  const B = makeBeats(audioJson);
  // sheet: { name, text } of the lyric sheet, only loaded when lyrics.json lines lack `section`
  const L = makeLyrics(lyricsJson, B, { sheetText: sheet?.text ?? null, sheetName: sheet?.name ?? '' });
  const T = buildTimeline(song.timeline, { lyrics: L, beats: B });
  layout = layout || song.defaultLayout || 'default';
  const design = song.layouts?.[layout] ?? song.design ?? { w: 1920, h: 1080 };
  if (song.layouts && !song.layouts[layout]) throw new Error(`unknown layout "${layout}" (have ${Object.keys(song.layouts).join(', ')})`);
  const W = design.w, H = design.h;
  for (const w of T.windows) if (!song.scenes[w.plate]) throw new Error(`timeline references unknown plate "${w.plate}"`);

  const env = { song, B, L, T, audio: audioJson, lyricsJson, W, H, fps, width, height, layout, duration: B.duration };

2	// Engine: wires audio.json + lyrics.json + the song's timeline into a pure render(t).
3	//
4	// A song module (see songs/<song>/video/song.js) provides:
5	//   design   { w, h }                 design coordinate space (1920x1080), or
6	//   layouts  { name: { w, h } }        several (e.g. '9x16', '16x9'); defaultLayout picks one
7	//   fonts    [{ family, weight, style? }]
8	//   timeline [spec entries]            see timeline.js
9	//   scenes   { plateId: { draw(ctx, S) } }
10	//   setup(env)                         optional; derive song-level curves (story clock...)
11	//   frame(ctx, t, env)                 composites one frame (scene, post, HUD, karaoke)
12	//   sheetCells(env)                    optional; extra contact-sheet cells [{ t, label }]
13	//
14	// Frames carry no state between calls: render(t) for any t, in any order, on any worker.
15	
16	import { makeBeats } from './beats.js';
17	import { makeLyrics } from './lyrics.js';
18	import { buildTimeline } from './timeline.js';
19	import { clamp } from './util.js';
20	
21	export function createEngine({ song, lyricsJson, audioJson, width, height, fps = 30, layout = null, sheet = null }) {
22	  const B = makeBeats(audioJson);
23	  // sheet: { name, text } of the lyric sheet, only loaded when lyrics.json lines lack `section`
24	  const L = makeLyrics(lyricsJson, B, { sheetText: sheet?.text ?? null, sheetName: sheet?.name ?? '' });
25	  const T = buildTimeline(song.timeline, { lyrics: L, beats: B });
26	  layout = layout || song.defaultLayout || 'default';
27	  const design = song.layouts?.[layout] ?? song.design ?? { w: 1920, h: 1080 };
28	  if (song.layouts && !song.layouts[layout]) throw new Error(`unknown layout "${layout}" (have ${Object.keys(song.layouts).join(', ')})`);
29	  const W = design.w, H = design.h;
30	  for (const w of T.windows) if (!song.scenes[w.plate]) throw new Error(`timeline references unknown plate "${w.plate}"`);
31	
32	  const env = { song, B, L, T, audio: audioJson, lyricsJson, W, H, fps, width, height, layout, duration: B.duration };
33	
34	  /** Scene context for time t in window w. Song code adds palette (P) and frame state (F). */
35	  env.sceneContext = (t, w = T.windowAt(t), extra = {}) => {
36	    const lt = t - w.start;
37	    return {
38	      t, fps: env.fps, win: w, lt, dur: w.dur, p: clamp(lt / w.dur), params: w.params, n: w.params.n ?? 1,
39	      env, B, L, T, W, H,
40	      /** progress through [a, b] seconds relative to song time */
41	      beat: B.beatPos(t),
42	      ...extra,
43	    };
44	  };
45	  env.windowAt = (t) => T.windowAt(t);
46	  env.scene = (plate) => song.scenes[plate];
47	
48	  song.setup?.(env);
49	
50	  function render(ctx, t) {
51	    ctx.setTransform(width / W, 0, 0, height / H, 0, 0);
52	    ctx.globalAlpha = 1;
53	    ctx.globalCompositeOperation = 'source-over';
54	    ctx.filter = 'none';
55	    ctx.letterSpacing = '0px';
56	    ctx.shadowBlur = 0;
57	    ctx.shadowColor = 'transparent';
58	    ctx.lineCap = 'butt';
59	    ctx.lineJoin = 'miter';
60	    ctx.setLineDash([]);
61	    ctx.save();
62	    song.frame(ctx, t, env);
63	    ctx.restore();
64	  }
65	
66	  function heroCells() {
67	    const cells = T.windows.map((w) => ({ t: T.heroTime(w), label: `${w.plate}${w.params.n ? ` n=${w.params.n}` : ''}`, plate: w.plate, id: w.id }));
68	    const extra = song.sheetCells?.(env) || [];
69	    return [...cells, ...extra].sort((a, b) => a.t - b.t);
70	  }
71	
72	  return { env, render, duration: B.duration, windows: T.windows, heroCells };
73	}
74	=== boot.js
75	// Page bootstrap shared by the preview and the headless render.
76	//   index.html?t=23            preview, scrubbed to 23 s
77	//   index.html?plate=hook&n=2  preview, jumped to that plate's hero time
78	//   index.html?render=1&w=540&h=960&fps=30&layout=9x16   headless mode: exposes window.__render
79	//   index.html?layout=16x9     preview the secondary layout
80	
81	import { createEngine } from './engine.js';
82	import { startPreview } from '../preview.js';
83	import { fmtTime } from './util.js';

85	async function fetchJSON(url) {
86	  const r = await fetch(url, { cache: 'no-store' });
87	  if (!r.ok) throw new Error(`fetch ${url}: ${r.status}`);
88	  return r.json();
89	}
90	
91	async function stylesheetsLoaded() {
92	  const links = [...document.querySelectorAll('link[rel="stylesheet"]')];
93	  await Promise.all(links.map((l) => (l.sheet ? null : new Promise((res) => {
94	    l.addEventListener('load', res, { once: true });
95	    l.addEventListener('error', res, { once: true });
96	  }))));
97	}
98	
99	/**
100	 * Load every font the song declares and verify a real face (not a fallback) is loaded.
101	 * document.fonts.check() returns true for undeclared families, so look the faces up.
102	 */
103	export async function loadFonts(fonts, sample = 'AaBbGg 0123456789:.,!?\'"()') {
104	  await stylesheetsLoaded();
105	  const specs = fonts.map((f) => `${f.style || 'normal'} ${f.weight || 400} 64px "${f.family}"`);
106	  await Promise.all(specs.map((s) => document.fonts.load(s, sample).catch(() => null)));
107	  await document.fonts.ready;
108	  const faces = [...document.fonts];
109	  const missing = [];
110	  for (const f of fonts) {
111	    const want = Number(f.weight || 400), style = f.style || 'normal';
112	    const ok = faces.some((ff) => {
113	      if (ff.family.replace(/["']/g, '') !== f.family || ff.status !== 'loaded') return false;
114	      if ((ff.style || 'normal') !== style && !(style === 'normal' && ff.style === 'normal')) return false;
115	      const ws = String(ff.weight).split(/\s+/).map(Number);
116	      return ws.length === 2 ? want >= ws[0] && want <= ws[1] : ws[0] === want;
117	    });
118	    if (!ok) missing.push(`${f.family} ${f.weight || 400}${f.style && f.style !== 'normal' ? ' ' + f.style : ''}`);
119	  }
120	  return missing;
121	}
122	
123	function toBase64(u8) {
124	  if (typeof u8.toBase64 === 'function') return u8.toBase64();
125	  let s = '';
126	  const CH = 0x8000;
127	  for (let i = 0; i < u8.length; i += CH) s += String.fromCharCode.apply(null, u8.subarray(i, i + CH));
128	  return btoa(s);
129	}
130	
131	function fnv(u8) {
132	  let h = 2166136261;
133	  for (let i = 0; i < u8.length; i += 7) h = Math.imul(h ^ u8[i], 16777619);
134	  return (h >>> 0).toString(16);
135	}
136	
137	
138	/**
139	 * The lyric sheet named in audio.json (`lyrics_sheet`), fetched only when some lyrics.json
140	 * line has no `section` (older alignment output). The name may be relative to the data
141	 * dir, to the song root (the video dir's parent: "suno_lyrics_v3_day.txt") or to the repo
142	 * root ("songs/2am-crying/suno_lyrics_v3_day.txt"); the first that loads wins.
143	 */
144	export async function loadSheet(lyricsJson, audioJson, { dataBase, base }) {
145	  const lines = lyricsJson.lines || [];
146	  if (lines.every((l) => l.section)) return null;
147	  const name = audioJson.lyrics_sheet || lyricsJson.lyrics_sheet;
148	  if (!name) {
149	    console.warn('[lyrics] lyrics.json lines have no "section" and audio.json names no lyrics_sheet to derive them from');
150	    return null;
151	  }
152	  const clean = String(name).replace(/^\/+/, '');
153	  const tries = (clean.includes('/') ? [new URL('/' + clean, location.origin), new URL('../' + clean, base)] : [new URL('../' + clean, base), new URL('/' + clean, location.origin)])
154	    .concat([new URL(clean, dataBase)]);
155	  for (const u of tries) {
156	    try {
157	      const r = await fetch(u, { cache: 'no-store' });
158	      if (r.ok) return { name: clean, text: await r.text(), url: String(u) };
159	    } catch { /* try the next */ }
160	  }
161	  console.warn(`[lyrics] lyrics_sheet "${name}" not found (tried ${tries.map(String).join(', ')})`);
162	  return null;
163	}
164	
165	export async function boot(song, { base }) {
166	  const q = new URLSearchParams(location.search);
167	  const render = q.has('render');
168	  const status = document.getElementById('status');
169	  try {
170	    const missing = await loadFonts(song.fonts || []);
171	    if (missing.length) {
172	      const msg = `fonts missing: ${missing.join(', ')}`;
173	      if (render) throw new Error(msg);
174	      console.warn(msg);
175	      if (status) status.textContent = msg;
176	    }
177	    const dataBase = new URL(q.get('data') || 'data/', base);
178	    const [lyricsJson, audioJson] = await Promise.all([
179	      fetchJSON(new URL('lyrics.json', dataBase)),
180	      fetchJSON(new URL('audio.json', dataBase)),
181	    ]);
182	    const sheet = await loadSheet(lyricsJson, audioJson, { dataBase, base });
183	    if (render) return bootRender(song, { lyricsJson, audioJson, q, sheet });
184	    return startPreview(song, { lyricsJson, audioJson, q, base, missingFonts: missing, sheet });
185	  } catch (e) {
186	    console.error(e);
187	    window.__render = { ready: false, error: String(e && e.stack || e) };
188	    if (status) status.textContent = 'ERROR: ' + (e && e.message || e);
189	    throw e;
190	  }
191	}
192	
193	function bootRender(song, { lyricsJson, audioJson, q, sheet = null }) {
194	  const layout = q.get('layout') || song.defaultLayout || null;
195	  const d = (layout && song.layouts?.[layout]) || song.design || { w: 1920, h: 1080 };
196	  const w = Number(q.get('w') || d.w / 2), h = Number(q.get('h') || d.h / 2), fps = Number(q.get('fps') || 30);
197	  const canvas = document.createElement('canvas');
198	  canvas.width = w; canvas.height = h;
199	  document.body.appendChild(canvas);
200	  const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d', { willReadFrequently: true, alpha: false });
201	  const engine = createEngine({ song, lyricsJson, audioJson, width: w, height: h, fps, layout, sheet });
202	  const draw = (t) => engine.render(ctx, t);
203	  window.__render = {
204	    ready: true,
205	    w, h, fps, layout: engine.env.layout,
206	    duration: engine.duration,
207	    windows: engine.windows.map((x) => ({ id: x.id, plate: x.plate, params: x.params, start: x.start, end: x.end, why: x.spec.at?.why ?? null, fromSection: x.spec.fromSection ?? null })),
208	    /** Where line sections came from ('lyrics.json' | 'sheet' | 'audio.json') and the expected section plates. */
209	    sections: { source: engine.env.L.sectionSource, list: [...new Set(engine.env.L.lines.map((l) => l.section))], expect: song.sectionPlates || [] },
210	    heroCells: () => engine.heroCells(),
211	    /** For a looping song: the time whose frame t repeats (null if the song does not say). */
212	    loopSource: (t) => (song.loopSource ? song.loopSource(engine.env, t) : null),
213	    /** Raw RGBA of the frame at t, base64. */
214	    frame(t) { draw(t); return toBase64(new Uint8Array(ctx.getImageData(0, 0, w, h).data.buffer)); },
215	    /** Several frames at once (fewer CDP round trips). */
216	    frames(ts) { return ts.map((t) => this.frame(t)); },
217	    png(t) { draw(t); return canvas.toDataURL('image/png'); },
218	    hash(t) { draw(t); return fnv(new Uint8Array(ctx.getImageData(0, 0, w, h).data.buffer)); },
219	    time(t, n = 5) { const t0 = performance.now(); for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) draw(t + i / fps); return (performance.now() - t0) / n; },
220	    /** Compose a contact sheet of cells [{t, label}] and return a PNG data URL. */
221	    sheet(cells, { cols = 4, cellW = 480, title = '' } = {}) {
222	      const cellH = Math.round((cellW * h) / w), labelH = 30, pad = 10, titleH = title ? 46 : 0;
223	      const rows = Math.ceil(cells.length / cols);
224	      const sc = document.createElement('canvas');
225	      sc.width = cols * (cellW + pad) + pad;
226	      sc.height = titleH + rows * (cellH + labelH + pad) + pad;
227	      const s = sc.getContext('2d');
228	      s.fillStyle = '#18181c';
229	      s.fillRect(0, 0, sc.width, sc.height);
230	      if (title) {
231	        s.fillStyle = '#e8e8ea';
232	        s.font = '600 22px "IBM Plex Mono", monospace';
233	        s.fillText(title, pad + 4, 31);
234	      }
235	      cells.forEach((c, i) => {
236	        const x = pad + (i % cols) * (cellW + pad), y = titleH + pad + Math.floor(i / cols) * (cellH + labelH + pad);
237	        draw(c.t);
238	        s.drawImage(canvas, x, y, cellW, cellH);
239	        s.fillStyle = '#e8e8ea';
240	        s.font = '600 16px "IBM Plex Mono", monospace';
241	        s.fillText(c.label, x + 2, y + cellH + 20);
242	        s.fillStyle = '#9a9aa2';
243	        s.textAlign = 'right';
244	        s.fillText(fmtTime(c.t), x + cellW - 2, y + cellH + 20);
245	        s.textAlign = 'left';
246	      });
247	      return sc.toDataURL('image/png');
248	    },
249	    env: engine.env,
250	  };
251	  return window.__render;
252	}
253	=== lyrics.js
254	// Lyric helpers over data/lyrics.json (lines -> words -> {w, start, end, conf, syl}).
255	// Matching is case- and punctuation-insensitive so anchors survive the real alignment.
256	
257	import { bisect } from './util.js';
258	import { parseSheet } from './sheet.js';
259	
260	/** Normalize for matching: lowercase, a.m. -> am, drop punctuation, collapse spaces. */
261	export function norm(s) {
262	  return String(s)
263	    .toLowerCase()
264	    .replace(/[‘’“”]/g, "'")
265	    .replace(/\b([ap])\.\s?m\.?/g, '$1m')
266	    .replace(/(\d)\s+([ap]m)\b/g, '$1$2')
267	    .replace(/[^a-z0-9:' ]+/g, ' ')
268	    .replace(/'/g, '')
269	    .replace(/\s+/g, ' ')
270	    .trim();
271	}
272	
273	/**
274	 * Section ids for lines that have none, from the lyric sheet: lines are matched to sheet
275	 * lines by normalized text, in sheet order (so repeated choruses land on their own
276	 * occurrence); a line the sheet does not have inherits the previous line's section.
277	 * Returns the number of lines that got a section.
278	 */
279	export function sectionsFromSheet(lines, sheetText) {
280	  const sheet = parseSheet(sheetText).map((x) => ({ ...x, n: norm(x.text) }));
281	  const order = [...lines].sort((a, b) => (a.i ?? 0) - (b.i ?? 0) || a.start - b.start);
282	  let cur = 0, prev = null, n = 0;
283	  for (const l of order) {
284	    let j = cur;
285	    while (j < sheet.length && sheet[j].n !== l.n) j++;
286	    const sec = j < sheet.length ? sheet[j].section : prev;
287	    if (j < sheet.length) cur = j + 1;
288	    if (!l.section && sec) { l.section = sec; n++; }
289	    prev = l.section || sec;
290	  }
291	  return n;
292	}
293	
294	/**
295	 * json: lyrics.json. beats: makeBeats(audio.json). sheetText: the lyric sheet, used only
296	 * when lines carry no `section`. Section precedence: the line's own `section` (the aligner
297	 * writes the sheet header's id), then the sheet, then audio.json's sections (a last
298	 * resort: detected sections are named s1, s2, ... and match no clip rule).
299	 */
300	export function makeLyrics(json, beats, { sheetText = null, sheetName = '' } = {}) {
301	  const lines = (json.lines || []).map((l, k) => {
302	    const words = (l.words && l.words.length ? l.words : fakeWords(l)).map((w, j) => ({ ...w, j }));
303	    return { ...l, i: l.i ?? k, words, n: norm(l.text) };
304	  }).sort((a, b) => a.start - b.start);
305	  lines.forEach((l, k) => { l.k = k; });
306	  const warn = (m) => { if (typeof console !== 'undefined') console.warn(m); };
307	  const missing = () => lines.filter((l) => !l.section).length;
308	  let sectionSource = 'lyrics.json';
309	  if (missing() && sheetText) {
310	    const n0 = missing();
311	    sectionsFromSheet(lines, sheetText);
312	    sectionSource = 'sheet';
313	    warn(`[lyrics] ${n0} of ${lines.length} lines have no "section" in lyrics.json: derived from the lyric sheet ${sheetName || ''}`.trim() +
314	      ` (${[...new Set(lines.map((l) => l.section))].join(', ')})${missing() ? `; ${missing()} still unassigned` : ''}`);
315	  }
316	  if (missing() && beats) {
317	    // last resort: section membership from audio.json
318	    const n0 = missing();
319	    for (const l of lines) {
320	      if (l.section) continue;
321	      const probe = l.start + Math.min(beats.beatDur, 0.3 * (l.end - l.start));
322	      l.section = beats.sectionAt(probe)?.name ?? l.section;
323	    }
324	    sectionSource = 'audio.json';
325	    const names = [...new Set(beats.sections.map((s) => s.name))];
326	    warn(`[lyrics] ${n0} line(s) have no "section" and no lyric sheet was loaded: using audio.json sections (${names.join(', ')})` +
327	      (names.every((x) => /^s\d+$/.test(x)) ? '. These are detected sections (s1, s2, ...): no clip rule keyed on a section name will match' : ''));
328	  }
329	  // Backing-vocal flags: words inside parentheses.
330	  for (const l of lines) {
331	    let depth = 0;
332	    for (const w of l.words) {
333	      const opens = (w.w.match(/\(/g) || []).length, closes = (w.w.match(/\)/g) || []).length;
334	      w.backing = depth > 0 || opens > 0;
335	      depth += opens - closes;
336	    }
337	  }
338	  const allWords = lines.flatMap((l) => l.words.map((w) => ({ ...w, line: l })));
339	
340	  function findLine({ match = null, section = null, nth = 0, after = null } = {}) {
341	    const m = match ? norm(match) : null;
342	    const hits = lines.filter((l) => (!section || l.section === section) && (!m || l.n.includes(m)) && (after == null || l.start >= after));
343	    return hits[nth] || null;
344	  }
345	  function lineAt(t) {
346	    const i = bisect(lines, t, (l) => l.start);
347	    return i >= 0 && t < lines[i].end ? lines[i] : null;
348	  }
349	  /**
350	   * The line the karaoke band should show at t: the most recent line that has started
351	   * (or starts within `lead`), held until the next one takes over or `hold` after its end.
352	   */
353	  function displayLine(t, lead = 0.25, hold = 0.6) {
354	    const i = bisect(lines, t + lead, (l) => l.start);
355	    if (i < 0) return null;
356	    const l = lines[i];
357	    if (t > l.end + hold) return null;
358	    return l;
359	  }
360	  function wordAt(t) {
361	    const l = lineAt(t);
362	    if (!l) return null;
363	    const j = bisect(l.words, t, (w) => w.start);
364	    return j >= 0 ? l.words[j] : null;
365	  }
366	  /** Find a word in a line by normalized text; `loose` falls back to the first word with digits. */
367	  function findWord(line, match, { nth = 0, loose = false } = {}) {
368	    if (!line) return null;
369	    const m = norm(match);
370	    const hits = line.words.filter((w) => norm(w.w) === m || norm(w.w).includes(m));
371	    if (hits[nth]) return hits[nth];
372	    if (loose) {
373	      const digits = line.words.filter((w) => /\d/.test(w.w));
374	      if (digits.length) return digits[0];
375	    }
376	    return null;
377	  }
378	  const wordsIn = (a, b) => allWords.filter((w) => w.start < b && w.end > a);
379	
380	  return { lines, allWords, extras: json.extras || [], notes: json.notes || '', sectionSource, findLine, lineAt, displayLine, wordAt, findWord, wordsIn, norm };
381	}
382	
383	function fakeWords(l) {
384	  const toks = String(l.text).split(/\s+/).filter(Boolean);
385	  const span = (l.end - l.start) || 1;
386	  return toks.map((w, k) => ({ w, start: l.start + (k * span) / toks.length, end: l.start + ((k + 1) * span) / toks.length, conf: 0 }));
387	}
388	=== timeline.js
389	// The edit: scene windows anchored to lyric lines (by content) or sections, snapped to
390	// the beat grid. A window runs from its anchor to the next window's anchor.
391	//
392	// spec entry:
393	//   { plate: 'hook', params: { n: 1 },
394	//     at: { section: 'chorus1' }                      // section start
395	//       | { line: 'Seven-thirty', section?: 'verse1', nth?: 0, word?: 'bath' }  // line (or word) start
396	//       | { time: 0 },                                // absolute (only for the very first window)
397	//       | { t, why },                                 // a time a spec *function* derived from lyric content
398	//     snap: 'downbeat' | 'beat' | 'half' | 'none',    // default 'downbeat'
399	//     offsetBeats: 0,                                 // nudge after snapping
400	//     hero: { line, word, section, offsetBeats } | number (fraction of window)  // contact-sheet time
401	//   }
402	
403	import { bisect } from './util.js';
404	
405	export function buildTimeline(spec, { lyrics, beats }) {
406	  // A spec may be a function of the data, so clips can be derived from lyric content.
407	  if (typeof spec === 'function') spec = spec({ lyrics, beats });
408	  const resolveAnchor = (at, label) => {
409	    if (at == null) throw new Error(`timeline: plate ${label} has no anchor`);
410	    if (typeof at.time === 'number') return at.time;
411	    if (typeof at.t === 'number') return at.t;   // derived from lyric content by a spec function
412	    if (at.line != null || at.word != null) {
413	      const line = lyrics.findLine({ match: at.line ?? null, section: at.section ?? null, nth: at.nth ?? 0 });
414	      if (!line) throw new Error(`timeline: plate ${label}: no lyric line matches ${JSON.stringify(at)}`);
415	      if (at.word) {
416	        const w = lyrics.findWord(line, at.word, { nth: at.wordNth ?? 0, loose: at.loose ?? false });
417	        if (!w) throw new Error(`timeline: plate ${label}: no word "${at.word}" in line "${line.text}"`);
418	        return w.start;
419	      }
420	      return line.start;
421	    }
422	    if (at.section) {
423	      const s = beats.section(at.section);
424	      if (!s) throw new Error(`timeline: plate ${label}: no section "${at.section}" in audio.json`);
425	      return s.start;
426	    }
427	    throw new Error(`timeline: plate ${label}: cannot resolve anchor ${JSON.stringify(at)}`);
428	  };
429	
430	  const windows = spec.map((e, idx) => {
431	    const label = `${e.plate}${e.params?.n ? `#${e.params.n}` : ''} (entry ${idx})`;
432	    const raw = resolveAnchor(e.at, label);
433	    let start = beats.snap(raw, e.snap ?? 'downbeat') + (e.offsetBeats || 0) * beats.beatDur;
434	    if (idx === 0) start = Math.min(start, 0);
435	    return { idx, id: `${e.plate}${e.params?.n ? e.params.n : ''}`, plate: e.plate, params: { ...(e.params || {}) }, anchor: raw, start, spec: e, label };
436	  });
437	  // Real (aligned) vocals don't land exactly on the grid, so two consecutive clauses can
438	  // snap to the same downbeat. Resolve collisions by tightening the snap for the later
439	  // window (beat, then half-beat), and as a last resort by placing it one beat after the
440	  // previous window. Windows stay monotonic and non-empty instead of throwing.
441	  for (let i = 1; i < windows.length; i++) {
442	    const prev = windows[i - 1], w = windows[i];
443	    if (w.start > prev.start) continue;
444	    const tries = [beats.snap(w.anchor, 'beat'), beats.snap(w.anchor, 'half'), w.anchor];
445	    let fixed = tries.find((t) => t > prev.start + 1e-6);
446	    if (fixed == null) fixed = prev.start + beats.beatDur;
447	    w.collided = { from: w.start, to: fixed };
448	    w.start = fixed;
449	  }
450	  for (let i = 0; i < windows.length; i++) {
451	    windows[i].end = i + 1 < windows.length ? windows[i + 1].start : beats.duration;
452	    if (!(windows[i].end > windows[i].start)) {
453	      throw new Error(`timeline: window ${windows[i].label} is empty or out of order (${windows[i].start.toFixed(3)} -> ${windows[i].end.toFixed(3)})`);
454	    }
455	  }
456	  const collided = windows.filter((w) => w.collided);
457	  if (collided.length && typeof console !== 'undefined') {
458	    console.warn(`[timeline] ${collided.length} window(s) moved off a shared downbeat: ` +
459	      collided.slice(0, 6).map((w) => `${w.label} ${w.collided.from.toFixed(2)}->${w.collided.to.toFixed(2)}`).join('; ') + (collided.length > 6 ? ' ...' : ''));
460	  }
461	  // Lines belong to the window that contains a point just inside their start (pickups
462	  // before a snapped downbeat still belong to the plate they open).
463	  for (const w of windows) w.lines = [];
464	  for (const l of lyrics.lines) {
465	    const probe = l.start + Math.min(beats.beatDur, 0.3 * (l.end - l.start));
466	    const w = windowAt(probe);
467	    if (w) w.lines.push(l);
468	  }
469	  for (const w of windows) {
470	    w.dur = w.end - w.start;
471	    w.line = (k) => w.lines[k] || null;
472	    /** Find a word by text in this window's lines: word(match, {line: k | 'text', nth}) */
473	    w.word = (match, o = {}) => {
474	      const pool = o.line == null ? w.lines : typeof o.line === 'number' ? [w.lines[o.line]].filter(Boolean)
475	        : w.lines.filter((l) => l.n.includes(lyrics.norm(o.line)));
476	      let n = o.nth ?? 0;
477	      for (const l of pool) {
478	        for (const wd of l.words) {
479	          const nw = lyrics.norm(wd.w), m = lyrics.norm(match);
480	          if (nw === m || nw.includes(m)) { if (n-- === 0) return wd; }
481	        }
482	      }
483	      if (o.loose) for (const l of pool) for (const wd of l.words) if (/\d/.test(wd.w)) return wd;
484	      if (o.required === false) return null;
485	      throw new Error(`plate ${w.label}: no word "${match}" in its lines (${w.lines.map((l) => l.text).join(' / ')})`);
486	    };
487	    /** Time of a word's start (snapped to the beat grid if snap is given). */
488	    w.wordTime = (match, o = {}) => {
489	      const wd = w.word(match, o);
490	      if (!wd) return o.fallback ?? w.start;
491	      return o.snap ? beats.snap(wd.start, o.snap) : wd.start;
492	    };
493	    w.findLine = (match) => w.lines.find((l) => l.n.includes(lyrics.norm(match))) || null;
494	  }
495	
496	  function windowAt(t) {
497	    const i = bisect(windows, t, (w) => w.start);
498	    return windows[Math.max(0, i)];
499	  }
500	
501	  function heroTime(w) {
502	    const h = w.spec.hero;
503	    if (h == null) return w.start + w.dur * 0.5;
504	    if (typeof h === 'number') return w.start + w.dur * h;
505	    if (typeof h === 'function') return h(w);
506	    let t;
507	    if (h.word) t = w.wordTime(h.word, { line: h.line, nth: h.nth, loose: h.loose });
508	    else if (h.line) { const l = w.findLine(h.line); if (!l) throw new Error(`hero: no line ${h.line} in ${w.label}`); t = l.start; }
509	    else t = w.start;
510	    return t + (h.offsetBeats || 0) * beats.beatDur + (h.offset || 0);
511	  }
512	
513	  return { windows, windowAt, heroTime };
514	}
515	=== util.js
516	// Math, easing, envelopes and seeded hashing. Everything here is pure: a frame is a
517	// function of song time, so nothing may carry state between calls.
518	
519	export const TAU = Math.PI * 2;
520	export const clamp = (x, a = 0, b = 1) => (x < a ? a : x > b ? b : x);
521	export const lerp = (a, b, k) => a + (b - a) * k;
522	export const invLerp = (a, b, x) => (b === a ? 0 : (x - a) / (b - a));
523	/** Progress of t through [a, b], clamped to 0..1. */
524	export const seg = (t, a, b) => clamp(invLerp(a, b, t));
525	export const smooth = (k) => { k = clamp(k); return k * k * (3 - 2 * k); };
526	/** 1 inside [a, b], with linear fades of fi / fo seconds at the edges. */
527	export const trap = (t, a, b, fi = 0.1, fo = 0.1) =>
528	  Math.min(fi > 0 ? clamp((t - a) / fi) : (t >= a ? 1 : 0), fo > 0 ? clamp((b - t) / fo) : (t < b ? 1 : 0));
529	
530	export const ease = {
531	  linear: (k) => k,
532	  inQuad: (k) => k * k,
533	  outQuad: (k) => 1 - (1 - k) * (1 - k),
534	  inOutQuad: (k) => (k < 0.5 ? 2 * k * k : 1 - Math.pow(-2 * k + 2, 2) / 2),
535	  inCubic: (k) => k * k * k,
536	  outCubic: (k) => 1 - Math.pow(1 - k, 3),
537	  inOutCubic: (k) => (k < 0.5 ? 4 * k * k * k : 1 - Math.pow(-2 * k + 2, 3) / 2),
538	  outQuart: (k) => 1 - Math.pow(1 - k, 4),
539	  inQuart: (k) => k * k * k * k,
540	  inExpo: (k) => (k <= 0 ? 0 : Math.pow(2, 10 * k - 10)),
541	  outExpo: (k) => (k >= 1 ? 1 : 1 - Math.pow(2, -10 * k)),
542	  inOutExpo: (k) => (k <= 0 ? 0 : k >= 1 ? 1 : k < 0.5 ? Math.pow(2, 20 * k - 10) / 2 : (2 - Math.pow(2, -20 * k + 10)) / 2),
543	  outBack: (k, s = 1.70158) => 1 + (s + 1) * Math.pow(k - 1, 3) + s * Math.pow(k - 1, 2),
544	  inBack: (k, s = 1.70158) => (s + 1) * k * k * k - s * k * k,
545	  outElastic: (k) => (k <= 0 ? 0 : k >= 1 ? 1 : Math.pow(2, -10 * k) * Math.sin((k * 10 - 0.75) * (TAU / 3)) + 1),
546	  outBounce: (k) => {
547	    const n = 7.5625, d = 2.75;
548	    if (k < 1 / d) return n * k * k;
549	    if (k < 2 / d) return n * (k -= 1.5 / d) * k + 0.75;
550	    if (k < 2.5 / d) return n * (k -= 2.25 / d) * k + 0.9375;
551	    return n * (k -= 2.625 / d) * k + 0.984375;
552	  },
553	};
554	/** Eased progress of t through [a, b]. */
555	export const eseg = (t, a, b, fn = ease.outCubic) => fn(seg(t, a, b));
556	
557	/**
558	 * Envelope that peaks (=1) at `at`: linear attack over `attack` seconds before it,
559	 * exponential decay with time constant `decay` after it. 0 far away.
560	 */
561	export function pulse(t, at, attack = 0.0, decay = 0.2) {
562	  const d = t - at;
563	  if (d < 0) return attack > 0 && d > -attack ? 1 + d / attack : 0;
564	  return Math.exp(-d / decay);
565	}
566	
567	/** Integer hash -> [0, 1). Stable across runs and machines (Mulberry32 finalizer). */
568	export function hash(i, salt = 0) {
569	  let x = (Math.imul((i | 0) ^ 0x9e3779b9, 0x85ebca6b) ^ Math.imul(salt | 0, 0xc2b2ae35)) >>> 0;
570	  x = Math.imul(x ^ (x >>> 16), 0x7feb352d) >>> 0;
571	  x = Math.imul(x ^ (x >>> 15), 0x846ca68b) >>> 0;
572	  x = (x ^ (x >>> 16)) >>> 0;
573	  return x / 4294967296;
574	}
575	/** Signed hash in [-1, 1). */
576	export const shash = (i, salt = 0) => hash(i, salt) * 2 - 1;
577	/** Hash of a string (for naming seeds by object id). */
578	export function strSeed(s) {
579	  let h = 2166136261;
580	  for (let i = 0; i < s.length; i++) h = Math.imul(h ^ s.charCodeAt(i), 16777619);
581	  return h >>> 0;
582	}
583	/** A deterministic generator for loops inside one frame (seed it per object, never per frame order). */
584	export function rng(seed) {
585	  let a = seed >>> 0 || 1;
586	  return () => {
587	    a = (a + 0x6d2b79f5) >>> 0;
588	    let t = a;
589	    t = Math.imul(t ^ (t >>> 15), t | 1);
590	    t ^= t + Math.imul(t ^ (t >>> 7), t | 61);
591	    return ((t ^ (t >>> 14)) >>> 0) / 4294967296;
592	  };
593	}
594	/** Smooth 1D value noise in [-1, 1]. */
595	export function noise1(x, salt = 0) {
596	  const i = Math.floor(x), f = x - i;
597	  const u = f * f * (3 - 2 * f);
598	  return lerp(shash(i, salt), shash(i + 1, salt), u);
599	}
600	
601	/** Index of the last element of sorted `arr` that is <= x (-1 if none). */
602	export function bisect(arr, x, key = null) {
603	  let lo = 0, hi = arr.length - 1, ans = -1;
604	  while (lo <= hi) {
605	    const mid = (lo + hi) >> 1;
606	    const v = key ? key(arr[mid]) : arr[mid];
607	    if (v <= x) { ans = mid; lo = mid + 1; } else hi = mid - 1;
608	  }
609	  return ans;
610	}
611	
612	/** True when the event at time `ev` belongs to the frame sampled at t (interval [t, t+1/fps)). */
613	export const inFrame = (ev, t, fps) => ev >= t - 1e-9 && ev < t + 1 / fps - 1e-9;
614	
615	/** 83.5 -> "1:23.50" */
616	export const fmtTime = (t) => {
617	  const m = Math.floor(t / 60), s = t - m * 60;
618	  return `${m}:${s.toFixed(2).padStart(5, '0')}`;
619	};
620	=== beats.js
621	// Beat grid helpers built from data/audio.json.
622	//   beatPos(t)   -> { beat (fractional index), i, phase, bar, barPhase, beatInBar }
623	//   beatTime(fi) -> seconds at a fractional beat index (extrapolates past the grid)
624	//   snap(t, mode) with mode 'beat' | 'half' (half-beat) | 'halfbar' | 'downbeat' | 'prevDownbeat' | 'none'
625	//   kickEnv / sinceKick / hatEnv for strobes; sectionAt(t)
626	
627	import { bisect, clamp } from './util.js';
628	
629	export function makeBeats(audio) {
630	  const bpm = audio.bpm || 120;
631	  const beatDur = 60 / bpm;
632	  const bpb = audio.beats_per_bar || 4;
633	  const beats = (audio.beats && audio.beats.length ? audio.beats : [0]).slice();
634	  const downbeats = audio.downbeats && audio.downbeats.length ? audio.downbeats.slice() : beats.filter((_, i) => i % bpb === 0);
635	  const kicks = (audio.kicks || []).slice().sort((a, b) => a - b);
636	  const hats = (audio.hats && audio.hats.length ? audio.hats : beats.map((b) => b + beatDur / 2)).slice().sort((a, b) => a - b);
637	  const sections = (audio.sections || []).slice();
638	  const duration = audio.duration || (beats.at(-1) + beatDur);
639	  // index of the first downbeat in the beat array, so bar numbers follow the downbeats
640	  const db0 = Math.max(0, beats.findIndex((b) => Math.abs(b - downbeats[0]) < beatDur / 4));
641	
642	  function beatTime(fi) {
643	    const n = beats.length;
644	    if (fi <= 0) return beats[0] + fi * beatDur;
645	    if (fi >= n - 1) return beats[n - 1] + (fi - (n - 1)) * beatDur;
646	    const i = Math.floor(fi), f = fi - i;
647	    return beats[i] + f * (beats[i + 1] - beats[i]);
648	  }
649	  function beatIndex(t) {
650	    const n = beats.length;
651	    if (t <= beats[0]) return (t - beats[0]) / beatDur;
652	    if (t >= beats[n - 1]) return n - 1 + (t - beats[n - 1]) / beatDur;
653	    const i = bisect(beats, t);
654	    return i + (t - beats[i]) / (beats[i + 1] - beats[i]);
655	  }
656	  function beatPos(t) {
657	    const beat = beatIndex(t);
658	    const i = Math.floor(beat);
659	    const phase = beat - i;
660	    const rel = beat - db0;
661	    const bar = Math.floor(rel / bpb);
662	    const barPhase = (rel / bpb) - bar;
663	    const beatInBar = ((i - db0) % bpb + bpb) % bpb;
664	    return { beat, i, phase, bar, barPhase, beatInBar };
665	  }
666	  function snap(t, mode = 'downbeat') {
667	    if (mode === 'none' || !mode) return t;
668	    const b = beatIndex(t);
669	    if (mode === 'beat') return beatTime(Math.round(b));
670	    if (mode === 'half') return beatTime(Math.round(b * 2) / 2);
671	    if (mode === 'downbeat') {
672	      const rel = (b - db0) / bpb;
673	      return beatTime(db0 + Math.round(rel) * bpb);
674	    }
675	    if (mode === 'halfbar') {
676	      const half = bpb / 2;
677	      return beatTime(db0 + Math.round((b - db0) / half) * half);
678	    }
679	    if (mode === 'prevDownbeat') {
680	      const rel = (b - db0 + 1e-6) / bpb;
681	      return beatTime(db0 + Math.floor(rel) * bpb);
682	    }
683	    throw new Error('unknown snap mode ' + mode);
684	  }
685	  const barStart = (t) => snap(t, 'prevDownbeat');
686	  const barDur = beatDur * bpb;
687	
688	  function lastKick(t) {
689	    const i = bisect(kicks, t + 1e-9);
690	    return i < 0 ? -Infinity : kicks[i];
691	  }
692	  const sinceKick = (t) => t - lastKick(t);
693	  /** 1 at a kick, decaying exponentially. */
694	  const kickEnv = (t, decay = 0.12) => { const d = sinceKick(t); return d < 0 || !isFinite(d) ? 0 : Math.exp(-d / decay); };
695	  function kickIndex(t) { return bisect(kicks, t + 1e-9); }
696	  function lastHat(t) { const i = bisect(hats, t + 1e-9); return i < 0 ? -Infinity : hats[i]; }
697	  const hatEnv = (t, decay = 0.06) => { const d = t - lastHat(t); return !isFinite(d) ? 0 : Math.exp(-d / decay); };
698	  /** Beat envelope independent of kicks (works in kick-less sections). */
699	  const beatEnv = (t, decay = 0.12) => { const p = beatPos(t); return Math.exp(-(p.phase * beatDur) / decay); };
700	
701	  function sectionAt(t) {
702	    for (const s of sections) if (t >= s.start && t < s.end) return s;
703	    return t < (sections[0]?.start ?? 0) ? sections[0] : sections.at(-1);
704	  }
705	  const section = (name) => sections.find((s) => s.name === name);
706	  const inSpans = (t, spans) => (spans || []).some((q) => t >= q.start && t < q.end);
707	
708	  return {
709	    bpm, beatDur, barDur, bpb, beats, downbeats, kicks, hats, sections, duration,
710	    beatTime, beatIndex, beatPos, snap, barStart, lastKick, sinceKick, kickEnv, kickIndex,
711	    lastHat, hatEnv, beatEnv, sectionAt, section, inSpans,
712	    quiet: audio.quiet || [], risers: audio.risers || [],
713	    /** Progress through a riser span covering t (0 if none). */
714	    riser(t) { for (const r of audio.risers || []) if (t >= r.start && t < r.end) return clamp((t - r.start) / (r.end - r.start)); return 0; },
715	  };
716	}
717	=== text.js
718	// Text helpers for canvas: font strings, fitting, tracking, wrapping.
719	
720	export const font = (weight, size, family, style = '') => `${style ? style + ' ' : ''}${weight} ${Math.max(1, size).toFixed(2)}px ${family}`;
721	
722	/** Largest font size (<= maxSize) at which `text` fits in maxW (and optionally maxH by cap height). */
723	export function fitSize(ctx, text, { weight = 900, family, maxW, maxH = Infinity, maxSize = 400, minSize = 8, tracking = 0 }) {
724	  ctx.save();
725	  ctx.font = font(weight, 100, family);
726	  ctx.letterSpacing = '0px';
727	  const w100 = ctx.measureText(text).width + tracking * 100 * Math.max(0, text.length - 1);
728	  ctx.restore();
729	  let s = w100 > 0 ? (maxW / w100) * 100 : maxSize;
730	  s = Math.min(s, maxSize, maxH / 0.72);
731	  return Math.max(minSize, s);
732	}
733	
734	/** Draw text with letter spacing (em fraction) using canvas letterSpacing. */
735	export function tracked(ctx, text, x, y, trackingEm = 0, size = null) {
736	  const prev = ctx.letterSpacing;
737	  if (trackingEm) {
738	    const px = size ?? parseFloat(ctx.font.match(/([\d.]+)px/)?.[1] || '16');
739	    ctx.letterSpacing = `${(trackingEm * px).toFixed(2)}px`;
740	  }
741	  ctx.fillText(text, x, y);
742	  ctx.letterSpacing = prev;
743	}
744	
745	export function measure(ctx, text, f) {
746	  if (f) ctx.font = f;
747	  return ctx.measureText(text).width;
748	}
749	
750	/** Greedy word wrap into lines no wider than maxW. */
751	export function wrap(ctx, text, maxW) {
752	  const words = String(text).split(/\s+/).filter(Boolean);
753	  const out = [];
754	  let cur = '';
755	  for (const w of words) {
756	    const t = cur ? cur + ' ' + w : w;
757	    if (ctx.measureText(t).width <= maxW || !cur) cur = t;
758	    else { out.push(cur); cur = w; }
759	  }
760	  if (cur) out.push(cur);
761	  return out;
762	}
763	
764	/** Rounded rectangle path. */
765	export function rrect(ctx, x, y, w, h, r) {
766	  r = Math.max(0, Math.min(r, w / 2, h / 2));
767	  ctx.beginPath();
768	  ctx.moveTo(x + r, y);
769	  ctx.arcTo(x + w, y, x + w, y + h, r);
770	  ctx.arcTo(x + w, y + h, x, y + h, r);
771	  ctx.arcTo(x, y + h, x, y, r);
772	  ctx.arcTo(x, y, x + w, y, r);
773	  ctx.closePath();
774	}
775	=== theme.js
776	// Palettes are plain objects of hex colors. A frame may blend two palettes (day/night
777	// inversion), so every consumer reads colors from the palette it is handed.
778	
779	import { clamp } from './util.js';
780	
781	const cache = new Map();
782	export function rgbOf(c) {
783	  if (Array.isArray(c)) return c;
784	  let v = cache.get(c);
785	  if (v) return v;
786	  const s = String(c).trim();
787	  if (s[0] === '#') {
788	    const h = s.length === 4 ? s.slice(1).split('').map((x) => x + x).join('') : s.slice(1, 7);
789	    v = [parseInt(h.slice(0, 2), 16), parseInt(h.slice(2, 4), 16), parseInt(h.slice(4, 6), 16)];
790	  } else {
791	    const m = s.match(/rgba?\(([^)]+)\)/);
792	    v = m ? m[1].split(',').slice(0, 3).map((x) => parseFloat(x)) : [255, 0, 255];
793	  }
794	  cache.set(c, v);
795	  return v;
796	}
797	export const rgb = (r, g, b) => `rgb(${r | 0},${g | 0},${b | 0})`;
798	export function rgba(c, a = 1) {
799	  const [r, g, b] = rgbOf(c);
800	  return `rgba(${r | 0},${g | 0},${b | 0},${clamp(a).toFixed(4)})`;
801	}
802	export function mix(c1, c2, k) {
803	  k = clamp(k);
804	  if (k === 0) return c1;
805	  if (k === 1) return c2;
806	  const a = rgbOf(c1), b = rgbOf(c2);
807	  return rgb(a[0] + (b[0] - a[0]) * k, a[1] + (b[1] - a[1]) * k, a[2] + (b[2] - a[2]) * k);
808	}
809	/** Blend every key present in palette a with the same key in b. */
810	export function blendPalette(a, b, k) {
811	  if (k <= 0) return a;
812	  if (k >= 1) return b;
813	  const out = {};
814	  for (const key of Object.keys(a)) out[key] = key in b && typeof a[key] === 'string' ? mix(a[key], b[key], k) : a[key];
815	  out.name = `${a.name}~${b.name}`;
816	  out.dayness = (a.dayness ?? 0) * (1 - k) + (b.dayness ?? 0) * k;
817	  return out;
818	}
819	/** Perceived luminance 0..1 (for picking legible overlays). */
820	export function luma(c) {
821	  const [r, g, b] = rgbOf(c);
822	  return (0.2126 * r + 0.7152 * g + 0.0722 * b) / 255;
823	}
824	=== sheet.js
825	// Suno lyric sheet -> section ids per line, for timing data that has no `section` field.
826	// Same rules as shared/align/common.py (parse_sheet / section_ids):
827	//   `[Section - notes]` headers name the section: the text before " - ", lowercased,
828	//   spaces dropped ("Verse 1" -> verse1); "Final Chorus" -> final; a plain name (no
829	//   trailing digit) that occurs more than once is numbered by occurrence (Chorus, Chorus
830	//   -> chorus1, chorus2). Blank lines are skipped, pure stage-direction parentheticals
831	//   ("(instrumental)") are dropped from a line, punctuation-only lines are skipped.
832	
833	const STAGE = /^(instrumental|.*\bsolo|.*\bbreak(down)?|beat drop|drop|fade( out)?|silence|pause|applause|music|outro music|intro music|end)$/i;
834	
835	export function sectionId(name) {
836	  const base = String(name).split(' - ')[0].trim().toLowerCase().replace(/\s+/g, '');
837	  return base === 'finalchorus' ? 'final' : base;
838	}
839	
840	/** Section id per header occurrence, in sheet order. */
841	export function sectionIds(headers) {
842	  const base = headers.map(sectionId);
843	  const count = {}, seen = {};
844	  for (const b of base) count[b] = (count[b] || 0) + 1;
845	  return base.map((b) => {
846	    if (count[b] > 1 && !/\d$/.test(b)) { seen[b] = (seen[b] || 0) + 1; return `${b}${seen[b]}`; }
847	    return b;
848	  });
849	}
850	
851	/** Sheet text -> [{ text, section, header }] lyric lines in sheet order. */
852	export function parseSheet(text) {
853	  const raw = [], headers = [];
854	  for (const r of String(text).split(/\r?\n/)) {
855	    let s = r.trim();
856	    if (!s) continue;
857	    if (s.startsWith('[') && s.endsWith(']')) { headers.push(s.slice(1, -1).split(' - ')[0].trim()); continue; }
858	    s = s.replace(/\(([^()]*)\)/g, (m, inner) => (STAGE.test(inner.trim().replace(/^[ .]+|[ .]+$/g, '')) ? '' : m)).replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim();
859	    if (!s || !/[a-z0-9]/i.test(s)) continue;
860	    raw.push({ text: s, h: headers.length - 1 });
861	  }
862	  const ids = sectionIds(headers);
863	  return raw.map((l) => ({ text: l.text, section: l.h >= 0 ? ids[l.h] : '', header: l.h >= 0 ? headers[l.h] : '' }));
864	}
