// Baby Clawd: the Claude Code mascot as a baby. Clawd is a blocky clay body (10 units wide,
// 6 tall) on four stubby legs with two arm nubs and tall rounded-rectangle eyes; the baby is
// chubbier and nearly square, with rounder corners, shorter legs, bigger and lower eyes with
// a big highlight, a single curl on top and blush. Night: a sleep sack that wraps the legs
// (a soft bag with a zipper). Day: a onesie band across the lower body with two snaps.
// 'naked' is the bare clay body (after the bath), 'towel' adds a cape. A pacifier for the
// quiet and sleeping moments. One arm nub always stretches toward a bottom corner of the
// frame, holding the phone (we see the nub, never the phone).
//
// drawBaby(ctx, o): o.x, o.y face centre, o.r scale (design px; the body is 2.5r wide), o.C
// colours (skin = clay, skinShade, skinLight, ink, line, mouth, white, cloth, clothShade,
// towel, stain, blush, tear, paci), plus the pose fields in BABY_DEFAULTS. No state, no
// randomness: the same o always draws the same pixels.

import { clamp, lerp, TAU } from '../../../shared/renderer/core/util.js';
import { mix, rgba } from '../../../shared/renderer/core/theme.js';

export const BABY_DEFAULTS = {
  r: 300, tilt: 0,
  outfit: 'onesie',          // 'onesie' | 'sack' | 'naked' | 'towel'
  eyes: 'open',              // 'open' | 'half' | 'closed' | 'squeeze' | 'wide' | 'focus'
  open: 1,                   // continuous eyelid 0..1 (overrides when < 1 for 'open'/'wide')
  lidSoft: false,            // heavy eyes without a lid line: the eye shortens from the top (drowsy, content)
  look: [0, 0],              // eye / highlight offset (-1..1)
  mouth: 'line',             // 'line' | 'o' | 'smile' | 'grin' | 'none' | 'press'
  mouthOpen: 1,
  brows: null,               // null | 'furrow' | 'up'  ('press' implies 'furrow')
  phoneArm: 'left',          // which bottom corner the phone nub reaches for
  free: { mode: 'down' },    // free nub: 'down' | 'wave' | 'up' | 'point' | 'hold' | 'none'
  stain: false, tears: 0, blush: 0,
  pacifier: 0,               // 0..1: the pacifier over the mouth (quiet and sleeping moments)
  flash: 0,                  // 0..1 hot-spot of the phone flash on the face
};

/** Geometry in units of r around the face centre (scenes place props and glows with it). */
export const BABY_GEOM = {
  body: { l: -1.25, r: 1.25, t: -1.0, b: 1.6, rad: 0.56, bulge: 0.05 },
  eye: { dx: 0.44, dy: 0.0, w: 0.31, h: 0.46, rad: 0.125 },
  mouthY: 0.47,
  cheek: { dx: 0.8, dy: 0.36 },
  arm: { x: 1.16, y: 0.46, t: 0.32 },
  legs: [-0.86, -0.38, 0.38, 0.86], legW: 0.3, legH: 0.3,
  sack: { t: 0.74, b: 2.08, w: 1.33 },
  band: 0.95,
};
const G0 = BABY_GEOM;

const ROSE = '#ee8a8a', SKY = '#8fd0f6';

/** The body outline: a rounded square whose sides bow out a little (chubby). */
function bodyPath(ctx, r) {
  const { l, r: rt, t, b, rad, bulge } = G0.body;
  const L = l * r, R = rt * r, T = t * r, B = b * r, k = rad * r, q = bulge * r;
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.moveTo(L + k, T);
  ctx.quadraticCurveTo(0, T - q, R - k, T);
  ctx.quadraticCurveTo(R, T, R, T + k);
  ctx.quadraticCurveTo(R + q, (T + B) / 2, R, B - k);
  ctx.quadraticCurveTo(R, B, R - k, B);
  ctx.quadraticCurveTo(0, B + q, L + k, B);
  ctx.quadraticCurveTo(L, B, L, B - k);
  ctx.quadraticCurveTo(L - q, (T + B) / 2, L, T + k);
  ctx.quadraticCurveTo(L, T, L + k, T);
  ctx.closePath();
}

function rr(ctx, x, y, w, h, k) {
  k = Math.max(0, Math.min(k, Math.abs(w) / 2, Math.abs(h) / 2));
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.moveTo(x + k, y);
  ctx.arcTo(x + w, y, x + w, y + h, k);
  ctx.arcTo(x + w, y + h, x, y + h, k);
  ctx.arcTo(x, y + h, x, y, k);
  ctx.arcTo(x, y, x + w, y, k);
  ctx.closePath();
}

/**
 * A nub from `a` to `b` (thickness t0 at the root, t1 at the tip). The root end sits inside
 * the body and is not outlined, so the nub grows out of the body silhouette.
 */
function nub(ctx, a, b, t0, t1, fill, line, lw, { tipRound = 0.35, rootOpen = true } = {}) {
  const dx = b[0] - a[0], dy = b[1] - a[1], d = Math.hypot(dx, dy) || 1;
  const nx = -dy / d, ny = dx / d;
  const h0 = t0 / 2, h1 = t1 / 2, c = Math.min(h1 * 2 * tipRound, d * 0.4);
  const ux = dx / d, uy = dy / d;
  const p = (s, h, along = 0) => [a[0] + ux * s * d + nx * h + ux * along, a[1] + uy * s * d + ny * h + uy * along];
  const A = p(0, h0), Bq = p(1, h1, -c), Cq = p(1, -h1, -c), D = p(0, -h0);
  const T1 = p(1, h1), T2 = p(1, -h1);
  const outline = () => {
    ctx.moveTo(A[0], A[1]);
    ctx.lineTo(Bq[0], Bq[1]);
    ctx.quadraticCurveTo(T1[0], T1[1], (T1[0] + T2[0]) / 2, (T1[1] + T2[1]) / 2);
    ctx.quadraticCurveTo(T2[0], T2[1], Cq[0], Cq[1]);
    ctx.lineTo(D[0], D[1]);
  };
  ctx.beginPath(); outline(); ctx.closePath();
  ctx.fillStyle = fill; ctx.fill();
  if (line) {
    ctx.beginPath(); outline();
    if (!rootOpen) ctx.closePath();
    ctx.strokeStyle = line; ctx.lineWidth = lw; ctx.stroke();
  }
}

function eyeAt(ctx, k, o, C, r) {
  const E = G0.eye;
  let mode = o.eyes;
  const open = clamp(o.open);
  if ((mode === 'open' || mode === 'wide' || mode === 'half') && open < 0.18) mode = 'closed';
  const lx = o.look[0] * 0.07 * r, ly = o.look[1] * 0.06 * r;
  const ex = k * E.dx * r, ey = E.dy * r;
  ctx.strokeStyle = C.ink; ctx.fillStyle = C.ink; ctx.lineCap = 'round'; ctx.lineJoin = 'round';
  if (mode === 'closed') {
    // sleeping: a soft downward arc
    ctx.lineWidth = 0.055 * r;
    ctx.beginPath(); ctx.moveTo(ex - 0.16 * r, ey + 0.02 * r); ctx.quadraticCurveTo(ex, ey + 0.15 * r, ex + 0.16 * r, ey + 0.02 * r); ctx.stroke();
    return;
  }
  if (mode === 'squeeze') {
    // squeezed shut: > <
    ctx.lineWidth = 0.062 * r;
    const w = 0.13 * r, h = 0.135 * r;
    ctx.beginPath(); ctx.moveTo(ex + k * w, ey - h); ctx.lineTo(ex - k * w * 0.9, ey); ctx.lineTo(ex + k * w, ey + h); ctx.stroke();
    return;
  }
  if (mode === 'focus') {
    // concentration: a narrow flat slit
    rr(ctx, ex - 0.16 * r + lx * 0.5, ey + 0.02 * r, 0.32 * r, 0.12 * r, 0.05 * r); ctx.fill();
    return;
  }
  const s = mode === 'wide' ? 1.14 : 1;
  const w = E.w * s * r, h = E.h * s * r;
  const x0 = ex - w / 2 + lx, y0 = ey - h / 2 + ly;
  const lid = mode === 'half' ? Math.min(open, 0.5) : open;
  const cut = y0 + h * (1 - lid);
  // a heavy lid: its edge bows up a little and droops toward the outer corner (drowsy, not cross)
  const droop = 0.09 * r * (1 - lid);
  const lidY = (x) => cut - 0.03 * r * (1 - lid) + droop * ((x - ex) * k) / w - 0.04 * r * (1 - lid) * (1 - ((x - ex) / (w * 0.6)) ** 2);
  if (o.lidSoft) {
    // soft heavy eyes: the eye shortens from the top, keeping its round corners, no lid line
    // (identical to the plain eye at lid 1, so a scene can open and close them without a jump)
    const hh = h * Math.max(lid, 0.22), yy = y0 + h - hh, kr = Math.min(E.rad * s * r, hh / 2);
    ctx.save();
    rr(ctx, x0, yy, w, hh, kr); ctx.fill(); ctx.clip();
    ctx.fillStyle = C.white || '#fff';
    const hr = w * 0.26 * Math.min(1, 0.45 + 0.55 * lid);
    ctx.beginPath(); ctx.arc(x0 + w * 0.33 + o.look[0] * 0.02 * r, yy + Math.max(hr * 0.6, hh * 0.27 + o.look[1] * 0.02 * r), hr, 0, TAU); ctx.fill();
    if (lid > 0.45) { ctx.beginPath(); ctx.arc(x0 + w * 0.7, y0 + h * 0.68, w * 0.11, 0, TAU); ctx.fill(); }
    ctx.restore();
    return;
  }
  ctx.save();
  if (lid < 1) {
    ctx.beginPath(); ctx.moveTo(x0 - 0.1 * r, lidY(x0 - 0.1 * r));
    for (let i = 1; i <= 8; i++) { const x = x0 - 0.1 * r + (w + 0.2 * r) * i / 8; ctx.lineTo(x, lidY(x)); }
    ctx.lineTo(x0 + w + 0.1 * r, y0 + 2 * h); ctx.lineTo(x0 - 0.1 * r, y0 + 2 * h); ctx.closePath(); ctx.clip();
  }
  rr(ctx, x0, y0, w, h, E.rad * s * r); ctx.fill();
  // the highlight: a big dot up and to the left, a small one below it
  const hx = x0 + w * 0.33 + o.look[0] * 0.02 * r, hy = Math.max(y0 + h * 0.27, lid < 1 ? lidY(x0 + w * 0.33) + w * 0.3 : -1e9) + o.look[1] * 0.02 * r;
  ctx.fillStyle = C.white || '#fff';
  ctx.beginPath(); ctx.arc(hx, hy, w * 0.26, 0, TAU); ctx.fill();
  ctx.beginPath(); ctx.arc(x0 + w * 0.7, y0 + h * 0.68, w * 0.11, 0, TAU); ctx.fill();
  if (mode === 'wide') { ctx.beginPath(); ctx.arc(x0 + w * 0.68, y0 + h * 0.3, w * 0.08, 0, TAU); ctx.fill(); }
  ctx.restore();
  if (lid < 1) {
    // the eyelid's edge, sagging a little
    ctx.strokeStyle = C.ink; ctx.lineWidth = 0.045 * r;
    ctx.beginPath();
    for (let i = 0; i <= 8; i++) { const x = x0 - 0.05 * r + (w + 0.1 * r) * i / 8; if (i) ctx.lineTo(x, lidY(x)); else ctx.moveTo(x, lidY(x)); }
    ctx.stroke();
  }
}

function brow(ctx, k, kind, C, r) {
  const E = G0.eye;
  const ex = k * E.dx * r, by = (E.dy - E.h / 2 - 0.11) * r;
  ctx.strokeStyle = C.ink; ctx.lineWidth = 0.045 * r; ctx.lineCap = 'round';
  ctx.beginPath();
  if (kind === 'furrow') { ctx.moveTo(ex + k * 0.16 * r, by - 0.05 * r); ctx.lineTo(ex - k * 0.1 * r, by + 0.04 * r); }
  else { ctx.moveTo(ex - 0.12 * r, by + 0.02 * r); ctx.quadraticCurveTo(ex, by - 0.07 * r, ex + 0.12 * r, by + 0.02 * r); }
  ctx.stroke();
}

function tears(ctx, phase, C, r) {
  const E = G0.eye;
  const col = C.tear || SKY;
  for (const k of [-1, 1]) {
    const x0 = k * (E.dx + 0.02) * r, y0 = (E.dy + E.h / 2 - 0.04) * r, y1 = 0.9 * r;
    const pts = [];
    for (let i = 0; i <= 6; i++) {
      const f = i / 6;
      pts.push([x0 + k * 0.05 * r * f + Math.sin(phase * TAU + i * 1.3 + k) * 0.022 * r * f, lerp(y0, y1, f)]);
    }
    const stroke = (w, c) => {
      ctx.strokeStyle = c; ctx.lineWidth = w; ctx.lineCap = 'round'; ctx.lineJoin = 'round';
      ctx.beginPath(); ctx.moveTo(pts[0][0], pts[0][1]);
      for (const p of pts.slice(1)) ctx.lineTo(p[0], p[1]);
      ctx.stroke();
    };
    stroke(0.13 * r, rgba(C.line, 0.6));
    stroke(0.1 * r, col);
    stroke(0.032 * r, rgba('#ffffff', 0.7));
    // a drop flicking off to the side
    for (const off of [0, 0.5]) {
      const ph = (phase * 1.6 + off + (k > 0 ? 0.25 : 0)) % 1;
      const dx = k * (0.22 + 0.5 * ph) * r, dy = (0.08 - 0.28 * Math.sin(Math.PI * ph) + 0.55 * ph * ph) * r;
      const s = (1 - 0.45 * ph) * r;
      ctx.fillStyle = col; ctx.strokeStyle = rgba(C.line, 0.5); ctx.lineWidth = 0.015 * r;
      const px = x0 + dx, py = y0 + dy, dr = 0.07 * s;
      ctx.beginPath(); ctx.moveTo(px, py - dr * 1.6); ctx.quadraticCurveTo(px + dr, py - dr * 0.2, px + dr * 0.9, py + dr * 0.25);
      ctx.arc(px, py + dr * 0.25, dr * 0.9, 0, Math.PI); ctx.quadraticCurveTo(px - dr, py - dr * 0.2, px, py - dr * 1.6); ctx.closePath();
      ctx.fill(); ctx.stroke();
    }
  }
}

function mouthAt(ctx, o, C, r) {
  const my = G0.mouthY * r;
  const mo = clamp(o.mouthOpen);
  ctx.strokeStyle = C.ink; ctx.lineWidth = 0.042 * r; ctx.lineCap = 'round'; ctx.lineJoin = 'round';
  ctx.fillStyle = C.mouth;
  switch (o.mouth) {
    case 'o': {
      const rx = (0.055 + 0.11 * mo) * r, ry = (0.04 + 0.16 * mo) * r, cy = my + 0.03 * r * mo;
      ctx.beginPath(); ctx.ellipse(0, cy, rx, ry, 0, 0, TAU); ctx.fill();
      if (mo > 0.55) {
        ctx.save(); ctx.clip();
        ctx.fillStyle = mix(ROSE, C.mouth, 0.25);
        ctx.beginPath(); ctx.ellipse(0, cy + ry * 0.75, rx * 0.7, ry * 0.42, 0, 0, TAU); ctx.fill();
        ctx.restore();
      }
      ctx.lineWidth = 0.035 * r;
      ctx.beginPath(); ctx.ellipse(0, cy, rx, ry, 0, 0, TAU); ctx.stroke();
      break;
    }
    case 'smile':
      ctx.beginPath(); ctx.moveTo(-0.12 * r, my - 0.02 * r); ctx.quadraticCurveTo(0, my + 0.1 * r, 0.12 * r, my - 0.02 * r); ctx.stroke();
      break;
    case 'grin': {
      const d = (0.1 + 0.14 * mo) * r;
      const path = () => { ctx.beginPath(); ctx.moveTo(-0.17 * r, my - 0.05 * r); ctx.lineTo(0.17 * r, my - 0.05 * r); ctx.bezierCurveTo(0.16 * r, my + d * 0.9, -0.16 * r, my + d * 0.9, -0.17 * r, my - 0.05 * r); ctx.closePath(); };
      path(); ctx.fill();
      ctx.save(); path(); ctx.clip();
      ctx.fillStyle = mix(ROSE, C.mouth, 0.2);
      ctx.beginPath(); ctx.ellipse(0, my + d * 0.7, 0.11 * r, 0.07 * r, 0, 0, TAU); ctx.fill();
      ctx.restore();
      ctx.lineWidth = 0.035 * r; path(); ctx.stroke();
      break;
    }
    case 'press':
      // straining: a flat, slightly crimped line
      ctx.beginPath(); ctx.moveTo(-0.11 * r, my); ctx.lineTo(-0.03 * r, my - 0.012 * r); ctx.lineTo(0.03 * r, my + 0.008 * r); ctx.lineTo(0.11 * r, my - 0.004 * r); ctx.stroke();
      break;
    case 'none':
      break;
    default:
      ctx.beginPath(); ctx.moveTo(-0.08 * r, my); ctx.lineTo(0.08 * r, my); ctx.stroke();
  }
}

function pacifier(ctx, a, C, r) {
  const my = G0.mouthY * r + 0.02 * r;
  ctx.save();
  ctx.globalAlpha *= clamp(a);
  // the shield over the mouth, a button, and the ring hanging from it
  ctx.lineWidth = 0.032 * r; ctx.strokeStyle = C.line;
  ctx.fillStyle = C.paci || '#7fcbc0';
  ctx.beginPath(); ctx.ellipse(0, my, 0.24 * r, 0.14 * r, 0, 0, TAU); ctx.fill(); ctx.stroke();
  ctx.fillStyle = rgba('#ffffff', 0.4);
  ctx.beginPath(); ctx.ellipse(-0.09 * r, my - 0.055 * r, 0.08 * r, 0.032 * r, -0.2, 0, TAU); ctx.fill();
  ctx.lineCap = 'round';
  // the ring, hinged on a button in the middle of the shield
  const ry = my + 0.11 * r, rad = 0.11 * r;
  ctx.strokeStyle = C.line; ctx.lineWidth = 0.085 * r;
  ctx.beginPath(); ctx.arc(0, ry, rad, 0, TAU); ctx.stroke();
  ctx.strokeStyle = C.paciRing || '#f6efe2'; ctx.lineWidth = 0.045 * r;
  ctx.beginPath(); ctx.arc(0, ry, rad, 0, TAU); ctx.stroke();
  ctx.fillStyle = mix(C.paci || '#7fcbc0', C.line, 0.3); ctx.strokeStyle = C.line; ctx.lineWidth = 0.02 * r;
  ctx.beginPath(); ctx.arc(0, my, 0.055 * r, 0, TAU); ctx.fill(); ctx.stroke();
  ctx.restore();
}

export function drawBaby(ctx, opts) {
  const o = { ...BABY_DEFAULTS, ...opts, free: { ...BABY_DEFAULTS.free, ...(opts.free || {}) } };
  const { r, C } = o;
  const B = G0.body;
  const lw = r * 0.034;
  const s = o.phoneArm === 'left' ? -1 : 1;
  const fs = -s;
  const clay = o.blush > 0 ? mix(C.skin, '#e2574a', 0.1 * clamp(o.blush)) : C.skin;
  const clayDk = C.skinShade, clayLt = C.skinLight || mix(C.skin, '#ffffff', 0.35);
  const line = C.line, cloth = C.cloth, clothShade = C.clothShade;
  const arm = G0.arm;
  ctx.save();
  ctx.translate(o.x, o.y);
  ctx.rotate(o.tilt);
  ctx.lineJoin = 'round';
  ctx.lineCap = 'round';

  // ---- behind the body: the towel cape, the curl, the legs ---------------------------------
  if (o.outfit === 'towel') {
    ctx.fillStyle = C.towel || '#f2f2f2';
    ctx.strokeStyle = rgba(line, 0.8); ctx.lineWidth = lw * 0.8;
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.moveTo(-1.0 * r, -0.85 * r);
    ctx.quadraticCurveTo(-1.95 * r, 0.6 * r, -2.05 * r, 2.3 * r);
    ctx.quadraticCurveTo(-1.0 * r, 2.05 * r, 0, 2.35 * r);
    ctx.quadraticCurveTo(1.0 * r, 2.05 * r, 2.05 * r, 2.3 * r);
    ctx.quadraticCurveTo(1.95 * r, 0.6 * r, 1.0 * r, -0.85 * r);
    ctx.closePath(); ctx.fill(); ctx.stroke();
    ctx.strokeStyle = rgba(mix(C.towel || '#f2f2f2', line, 0.3), 0.6); ctx.lineWidth = lw * 0.6;
    for (const k of [-1, 1]) { ctx.beginPath(); ctx.moveTo(k * 1.3 * r, 0.2 * r); ctx.quadraticCurveTo(k * 1.62 * r, 1.2 * r, k * 1.6 * r, 2.1 * r); ctx.stroke(); }
  }
  // a single curl on top, sprouting from behind the top edge
  {
    const path = () => {
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.moveTo(-0.04 * r, -0.92 * r);
      ctx.bezierCurveTo(-0.08 * r, -1.22 * r, 0.12 * r, -1.42 * r, 0.27 * r, -1.3 * r);
      ctx.bezierCurveTo(0.38 * r, -1.2 * r, 0.27 * r, -1.06 * r, 0.16 * r, -1.13 * r);
    };
    ctx.lineCap = 'round';
    path(); ctx.strokeStyle = line; ctx.lineWidth = 0.13 * r; ctx.stroke();
    path(); ctx.strokeStyle = clay; ctx.lineWidth = 0.13 * r - 2 * lw; ctx.stroke();
  }
  if (o.outfit !== 'sack') {
    ctx.fillStyle = clayDk; ctx.strokeStyle = line; ctx.lineWidth = lw * 0.9;
    for (const lx of G0.legs) {
      rr(ctx, (lx - G0.legW / 2) * r, (B.b - 0.12) * r, G0.legW * r, (G0.legH + 0.12) * r, 0.08 * r);
      ctx.fill(); ctx.stroke();
    }
  }

  // ---- the body -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
  bodyPath(ctx, r);
  ctx.fillStyle = clay;
  ctx.fill();
  ctx.save();
  bodyPath(ctx, r);
  ctx.clip();
  // flat clay shading: a light patch up and left, a darker band low on the body
  ctx.fillStyle = rgba(clayLt, 0.42);
  ctx.beginPath(); ctx.ellipse(-0.55 * r, -0.6 * r, 0.52 * r, 0.22 * r, -0.12, 0, TAU); ctx.fill();
  if (o.outfit === 'naked' || o.outfit === 'towel') {
    ctx.fillStyle = rgba(clayDk, 0.55);
    ctx.fillRect(-2 * r, 1.08 * r, 4 * r, 2 * r);
  }
  if (o.flash > 0) {
    const g = ctx.createRadialGradient(-0.1 * r, -0.05 * r, 0, -0.1 * r, -0.05 * r, 1.25 * r);
    g.addColorStop(0, rgba('#ffffff', 0.5 * o.flash));
    g.addColorStop(1, rgba('#ffffff', 0));
    ctx.fillStyle = g;
    ctx.fillRect(-2 * r, -2 * r, 4 * r, 4 * r);
  }
  if (o.outfit === 'onesie') {
    // the onesie: a cloth band across the lower body, two snaps
    const y = G0.band * r;
    ctx.fillStyle = cloth;
    ctx.beginPath(); ctx.moveTo(-2 * r, y + 0.02 * r); ctx.quadraticCurveTo(0, y - 0.05 * r, 2 * r, y + 0.02 * r); ctx.lineTo(2 * r, 3 * r); ctx.lineTo(-2 * r, 3 * r); ctx.closePath(); ctx.fill();
    ctx.fillStyle = rgba(clothShade, 0.7);
    ctx.fillRect(-2 * r, (B.b - 0.13) * r, 4 * r, r);
    ctx.strokeStyle = line; ctx.lineWidth = lw * 0.9;
    ctx.beginPath(); ctx.moveTo(-2 * r, y + 0.02 * r); ctx.quadraticCurveTo(0, y - 0.05 * r, 2 * r, y + 0.02 * r); ctx.stroke();
    for (const k of [-1, 1]) {
      ctx.fillStyle = mix(cloth, '#9aa3ad', 0.45);
      ctx.beginPath(); ctx.arc(k * 0.2 * r, (B.b - 0.24) * r, 0.055 * r, 0, TAU); ctx.fill();
      ctx.lineWidth = lw * 0.55; ctx.stroke();
      ctx.fillStyle = rgba('#ffffff', 0.8);
      ctx.beginPath(); ctx.arc(k * 0.2 * r - 0.015 * r, (B.b - 0.255) * r, 0.018 * r, 0, TAU); ctx.fill();
    }
  }
  if (o.stain) {
    const st = C.stain || '#e9dfc8';
    const blobs = [[-0.42, 0.98, 0.17], [-0.12, 1.12, 0.12], [0.24, 0.96, 0.1], [-0.25, 1.33, 0.14], [0.5, 1.18, 0.08], [0.05, 0.82, 0.07]];
    ctx.fillStyle = rgba(st, 0.95);
    for (const [sx, sy, sr] of blobs) { ctx.beginPath(); ctx.ellipse(sx * r, sy * r, sr * r * 1.25, sr * r, 0.4, 0, TAU); ctx.fill(); }
    ctx.strokeStyle = rgba(mix(st, line, 0.35), 0.55); ctx.lineWidth = lw * 0.55;
    for (const [sx, sy, sr] of blobs.slice(0, 4)) { ctx.beginPath(); ctx.ellipse(sx * r, sy * r, sr * r * 1.25, sr * r, 0.4, 0, TAU); ctx.stroke(); }
    // a drip running down from the mouth
    ctx.fillStyle = rgba(st, 0.95);
    rr(ctx, 0.03 * r, (G0.mouthY + 0.04) * r, 0.06 * r, 0.18 * r, 0.03 * r); ctx.fill();
    ctx.beginPath(); ctx.arc(0.06 * r, (G0.mouthY + 0.23) * r, 0.05 * r, 0, TAU); ctx.fill();
  }
  ctx.restore();
  bodyPath(ctx, r);
  ctx.strokeStyle = line; ctx.lineWidth = lw;
  ctx.stroke();

  // ---- the sleep sack: a soft bag over the lower body and the legs, zipped up the middle ---
  if (o.outfit === 'sack') {
    const S = G0.sack;
    const sackPath = () => {
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.moveTo(-S.w * r * 0.97, S.t * r);
      ctx.quadraticCurveTo(0, (S.t - 0.07) * r, S.w * r * 0.97, S.t * r);
      ctx.bezierCurveTo((S.w + 0.12) * r, (S.t + 0.5) * r, (S.w + 0.1) * r, (S.b - 0.35) * r, (S.w - 0.25) * r, S.b * r);
      ctx.quadraticCurveTo(0, (S.b + 0.08) * r, -(S.w - 0.25) * r, S.b * r);
      ctx.bezierCurveTo(-(S.w + 0.1) * r, (S.b - 0.35) * r, -(S.w + 0.12) * r, (S.t + 0.5) * r, -S.w * r * 0.97, S.t * r);
      ctx.closePath();
    };
    sackPath(); ctx.fillStyle = cloth; ctx.fill();
    ctx.save(); sackPath(); ctx.clip();
    ctx.fillStyle = rgba(clothShade, 0.75);
    ctx.beginPath(); ctx.ellipse(1.55 * r, 1.5 * r, 0.5 * r, 1.2 * r, 0, 0, TAU); ctx.fill();
    // the binding along the top hem
    ctx.strokeStyle = rgba(clothShade, 1); ctx.lineWidth = 0.09 * r;
    ctx.beginPath(); ctx.moveTo(-S.w * r, (S.t + 0.04) * r); ctx.quadraticCurveTo(0, (S.t - 0.03) * r, S.w * r, (S.t + 0.04) * r); ctx.stroke();
    // zipper: a seam with teeth and the pull tab at the top
    ctx.strokeStyle = rgba(mix(clothShade, line, 0.35), 0.95); ctx.lineWidth = lw * 0.75;
    ctx.beginPath(); ctx.moveTo(0.06 * r, (S.t + 0.06) * r); ctx.quadraticCurveTo(0.1 * r, (S.t + 0.7) * r, 0.04 * r, (S.b + 0.1) * r); ctx.stroke();
    ctx.lineWidth = lw * 0.45;
    for (let i = 1; i < 11; i++) {
      const f = i / 11, zy = lerp(S.t + 0.08, S.b, f) * r, zx = (0.06 + 0.04 * Math.sin(Math.PI * f)) * r;
      ctx.beginPath(); ctx.moveTo(zx - 0.03 * r, zy); ctx.lineTo(zx + 0.03 * r, zy); ctx.stroke();
    }
    ctx.restore();
    sackPath(); ctx.strokeStyle = line; ctx.lineWidth = lw; ctx.stroke();
    ctx.fillStyle = mix(clothShade, '#ffffff', 0.25);
    rr(ctx, 0.0, (S.t + 0.06) * r, 0.12 * r, 0.2 * r, 0.04 * r); ctx.fill();
    ctx.strokeStyle = line; ctx.lineWidth = lw * 0.6; ctx.stroke();
  }

  // ---- the phone nub: stretched to the bottom corner, the phone out of frame -------------------
  const root = (k) => [k * arm.x * r, arm.y * r];
  nub(ctx, root(s), [s * 2.0 * r, 3.6 * r], arm.t * 1.05 * r, arm.t * 2.1 * r, clay, line, lw);

  // ---- the face -------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  // blush: always a little (a baby), more on demand
  const bl = 0.32 + 0.68 * clamp(o.blush);
  for (const k of [-1, 1]) {
    const bx = k * G0.cheek.dx * r, by = G0.cheek.dy * r;
    ctx.fillStyle = rgba(C.blush || ROSE, 0.55 * bl);
    ctx.beginPath(); ctx.ellipse(bx, by, 0.17 * r, 0.09 * r, 0, 0, TAU); ctx.fill();
    if (o.blush > 0.6) {
      ctx.strokeStyle = rgba(mix(C.blush || ROSE, line, 0.35), 0.8); ctx.lineWidth = 0.022 * r;
      for (let i = 0; i < 3; i++) { ctx.beginPath(); ctx.moveTo(bx + (i - 1.4) * 0.075 * r, by + 0.045 * r); ctx.lineTo(bx + (i - 0.9) * 0.075 * r, by - 0.045 * r); ctx.stroke(); }
    }
  }
  for (const k of [-1, 1]) eyeAt(ctx, k, o, C, r);
  const brows = o.brows || (o.mouth === 'press' ? 'furrow' : null);
  if (brows) for (const k of [-1, 1]) brow(ctx, k, brows, C, r);
  if (o.tears > 0) tears(ctx, o.tears, C, r);
  if (!(o.pacifier >= 0.5)) mouthAt(ctx, o, C, r);
  if (o.pacifier > 0) pacifier(ctx, o.pacifier, C, r);

  // ---- the free nub ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  const f = o.free;
  const t = arm.t * r;
  if (f.mode === 'down') {
    nub(ctx, root(fs), [fs * 1.62 * r, 0.82 * r], t, t, clay, line, lw);
  } else if (f.mode === 'wave' || f.mode === 'up') {
    const a = f.angle ?? 0;
    const lift = f.mode === 'wave' ? 0.95 + 0.4 * Math.sin(a) : 1.2 + 0.25 * Math.sin(a);
    const len = 0.62 * r;
    nub(ctx, root(fs), [fs * (arm.x * r + Math.cos(lift) * len), arm.y * r - Math.sin(lift) * len], t, t * 1.05, clay, line, lw);
  } else if (f.mode === 'point') {
    // the nub coming at the lens: it grows, its flat end toward us
    const k = clamp(f.reach ?? 1);
    const hx = lerp(fs * 1.5, fs * 0.3, k) * r, hy = lerp(0.9, 1.0, k) * r, hr = lerp(0.2, 0.7, k) * r;
    nub(ctx, root(fs), [hx, hy], t, hr * 1.6, clay, line, lw);
    rr(ctx, hx - hr, hy - hr * 0.9, 2 * hr, 1.8 * hr, hr * 0.45);
    ctx.fillStyle = mix(clay, '#ffffff', 0.08); ctx.fill(); ctx.strokeStyle = line; ctx.lineWidth = lw; ctx.stroke();
  } else if (f.mode === 'hold') {
    // the nub reaches the point the scene gives (head-relative units); the scene draws the prop
    const hx = (f.at?.[0] ?? fs * 0.6) * r, hy = (f.at?.[1] ?? 1.2) * r;
    nub(ctx, root(fs), [hx, hy], t, t * 1.15, clay, line, lw);
  }
  ctx.restore();
}

/** Colour set for the baby from a graded palette (G) or the flash palette. */
export function babyColors(G, outfit = 'onesie') {
  return {
    skin: G.skin, skinShade: G.skinShade, skinLight: G.skinLight, hair: G.hair, ink: G.ink, line: G.line, mouth: G.mouth, white: G.white,
    cloth: outfit === 'sack' ? G.sack : G.onesie, clothShade: outfit === 'sack' ? G.sackShade : G.onesieShade,
    towel: G.towel, stain: G.stain, blush: G.blush, tear: G.tear, paci: G.paci, paciRing: G.paciRing,
  };
}

// Props and rooms, drawn in camera space (1080x1920, the phone's front camera). Rooms
// paint well past the frame edges so a tilted or shaken camera never shows a border.

import { clamp, lerp, TAU, hash, ease } from '../../../shared/renderer/core/util.js';
import { mix, rgba } from '../../../shared/renderer/core/theme.js';
import { rrect } from '../../../shared/renderer/core/text.js';

export const CW = 1080, CH = 1920;
const BIG = { x: -900, y: -900, w: CW + 1800, h: CH + 1800 };

export function fillAll(ctx, color) {
  ctx.fillStyle = color;
  ctx.fillRect(BIG.x, BIG.y, BIG.w, BIG.h);
}

/** Wall with a soft light falloff (brighter toward the window side). */
export function wall(ctx, G, { lightX = 200, lightY = 300, glow = 0.18 } = {}) {
  fillAll(ctx, G.wall);
  const g = ctx.createRadialGradient(lightX, lightY, 50, lightX, lightY, 1500);
  g.addColorStop(0, rgba('#ffffff', glow * (1 - G.light.night)));
  g.addColorStop(1, rgba('#ffffff', 0));
  ctx.fillStyle = g;
  ctx.fillRect(BIG.x, BIG.y, BIG.w, BIG.h);
}

/** A window with curtains; the sky colour follows the time of day. */
export function windowPane(ctx, G, { x = 70, y = 230, w = 360, h = 520, curtains = true, blinds = 0 } = {}) {
  ctx.save();
  ctx.fillStyle = G.trim;
  ctx.fillRect(x - 22, y - 22, w + 44, h + 44);
  const sky = ctx.createLinearGradient(0, y, 0, y + h);
  sky.addColorStop(0, G.sky);
  sky.addColorStop(1, mix(G.sky, G.window, 0.7));
  ctx.fillStyle = sky;
  ctx.fillRect(x, y, w, h);
  if (blinds > 0) {
    ctx.fillStyle = rgba(G.trim, 0.85);
    for (let i = 0; i < 14; i++) ctx.fillRect(x, y + i * (h / 14), w, (h / 14) * blinds * 0.55);
  }
  ctx.fillStyle = G.trim;
  ctx.fillRect(x + w / 2 - 8, y, 16, h);
  ctx.fillRect(x, y + h * 0.45 - 8, w, 16);
  if (curtains) {
    ctx.fillStyle = G.curtain;
    for (const [cx, dir] of [[x - 60, 1], [x + w + 60, -1]]) {
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.moveTo(cx - dir * 30, y - 60);
      ctx.lineTo(cx + dir * 110, y - 60);
      ctx.quadraticCurveTo(cx + dir * 60, y + h * 0.5, cx + dir * 120, y + h + 80);
      ctx.lineTo(cx - dir * 30, y + h + 80);
      ctx.closePath();
      ctx.fill();
    }
  }
  ctx.restore();
}

/** Crib seen from inside: the far rail and slats behind the baby, mattress below. */
export function cribBack(ctx, G, { railY = 760, bottomY = 1560, gap = 92, slatW = 34, x0 = -300, x1 = 1380, shade = 1 } = {}) {
  ctx.save();
  for (let x = x0; x < x1; x += gap) {
    ctx.fillStyle = G.crib;
    ctx.fillRect(x, railY, slatW, bottomY - railY);
    ctx.fillStyle = rgba(G.cribShade, 0.8 * shade);
    ctx.fillRect(x + slatW * 0.62, railY, slatW * 0.38, bottomY - railY);
  }
  ctx.fillStyle = G.crib;
  rrect(ctx, x0 - 40, railY - 46, x1 - x0 + 80, 58, 18); ctx.fill();
  ctx.fillStyle = rgba(G.cribShade, 0.9);
  ctx.fillRect(x0 - 40, railY + 2, x1 - x0 + 80, 10);
  ctx.fillStyle = G.sheet;
  ctx.fillRect(BIG.x, bottomY, BIG.w, 1600);
  ctx.fillStyle = rgba(G.sheetShade, 0.7);
  ctx.fillRect(BIG.x, bottomY, BIG.w, 26);
  ctx.restore();
}

/** Doorway on the far wall; `open` 0..1 widens the lit gap. Returns the light rect. */
export function doorway(ctx, G, { x = 690, y = 120, w = 300, h = 760, open = 1, hall = '#ffcf8a', hallAlpha = 1 } = {}) {
  ctx.save();
  ctx.fillStyle = G.trim;
  ctx.fillRect(x - 24, y - 24, w + 48, h + 24);
  const gw = w * clamp(open);
  const g = ctx.createLinearGradient(x, 0, x + gw, 0);
  g.addColorStop(0, rgba(hall, 0.95 * hallAlpha));
  g.addColorStop(1, rgba(mix(hall, '#ffffff', 0.3), 0.85 * hallAlpha));
  ctx.fillStyle = G.doorDark;
  ctx.fillRect(x, y, w, h);
  ctx.fillStyle = g;
  ctx.fillRect(x, y, gw, h);
  // the door leaf, swung open toward the room
  ctx.fillStyle = G.door;
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.moveTo(x + gw, y);
  ctx.lineTo(x + w + 10, y - 20);
  ctx.lineTo(x + w + 10, y + h + 30);
  ctx.lineTo(x + gw, y + h);
  ctx.closePath();
  ctx.fill();
  ctx.restore();
  return { x, y, w: gw, h };
}

/** Light spill on the floor/wall from an open door at night. */
export function spill(ctx, x, y, w, h, color, a = 0.25) {
  const g = ctx.createRadialGradient(x + w / 2, y + h, 20, x + w / 2, y + h, h * 1.1);
  g.addColorStop(0, rgba(color, a));
  g.addColorStop(1, rgba(color, 0));
  ctx.fillStyle = g;
  ctx.fillRect(x - h, y - h * 0.2, w + 2 * h, h * 1.6);
}

/**
 * Adult Clawd (the parent): the classic proportions, a 10u x 6u clay block on four legs with
 * two arm nubs and tall slit eyes. (x, y) = the ground point between the feet; u = unit.
 *   pose 'stand' | 'sit' (on the floor, legs out toward us) | 'slump' (in a chair, tipped back)
 *   eyes 'open' | 'tired' (heavy lids) | 'closed' | null (a pure silhouette)
 *   mouth 'o' (asleep, breathing: breath 0..1) | 'line' | null
 * color fills everything when there is no `clay` (a night silhouette); rim = a rim-light colour.
 * Close-ups (the bridge, held in the parent's arms): h = body height in u (default 6; the face
 * keeps its place under the top edge), arms = null leaves the side nubs off, shine adds
 * highlights to 'open' eyes and look = [x, y] (-1..1) shifts them, shoulder = { k, q, at } (in u)
 * rounds the top corners (radius k) and flares the sides out by q over `at` below them: the
 * body seen from a phone held low, a rounded silhouette instead of a slab.
 */
export function adultClawd(ctx, { x, y, u, pose = 'stand', color = '#14151b', clay = null, clayDk = null, ink = '#1e1412', rim = null, eyes = null, eyeColor = null, lean = 0, mouth = null, breath = 0.5, arms = [0.25, 0.25], h = 6, look = null, shine = null, shoulder = null }) {
  ctx.save();
  ctx.translate(x, y);
  ctx.rotate(lean);
  const fill = clay || color, dk = clayDk || (clay ? mix(clay, '#000000', 0.25) : color);
  const lw = clay ? Math.max(2, u * 0.16) : 0;
  const stroke = () => { if (lw) { ctx.strokeStyle = ink; ctx.lineWidth = lw; ctx.lineJoin = 'round'; ctx.stroke(); } };
  const legH = pose === 'stand' ? 2.2 : 0;
  const top = -(h + legH) * u, bot = -legH * u;
  // arm nubs (behind the body), raise angles in radians
  if (arms) for (const k of [-1, 1]) {
    const a = arms[k < 0 ? 0 : 1];
    ctx.save();
    ctx.translate(k * 4.9 * u, top + 3.5 * u);
    ctx.rotate(k < 0 ? a : -a);
    rrect(ctx, k < 0 ? -2.2 * u : 0, -0.5 * u, 2.2 * u, u, 0.2 * u);
    ctx.fillStyle = fill; ctx.fill(); stroke();
    ctx.restore();
  }
  // legs: standing under the body; sitting, the feet point at us below the front edge
  ctx.fillStyle = dk;
  for (const lx of [-4, -2, 1, 3]) {
    if (pose === 'stand') { rrect(ctx, lx * u, -2.4 * u, u, 2.4 * u, 0.15 * u); ctx.fill(); stroke(); }
    else { rrect(ctx, (lx - 0.1) * u, bot - 0.5 * u, 1.2 * u, 1.1 * u, 0.3 * u); ctx.fill(); stroke(); }
  }

export function adultClawd(ctx, { x, y, u, pose = 'stand', color = '#14151b', clay = null, clayDk = null, ink = '#1e1412', rim = null, eyes = null, eyeColor = null, lean = 0, mouth = null, breath = 0.5, arms = [0.25, 0.25], h = 6, look = null, shine = null, shoulder = null }) {
  ctx.save();
  ctx.translate(x, y);
  ctx.rotate(lean);
  const fill = clay || color, dk = clayDk || (clay ? mix(clay, '#000000', 0.25) : color);
  const lw = clay ? Math.max(2, u * 0.16) : 0;
  const stroke = () => { if (lw) { ctx.strokeStyle = ink; ctx.lineWidth = lw; ctx.lineJoin = 'round'; ctx.stroke(); } };
  const legH = pose === 'stand' ? 2.2 : 0;
  const top = -(h + legH) * u, bot = -legH * u;
  // arm nubs (behind the body), raise angles in radians
  if (arms) for (const k of [-1, 1]) {
    const a = arms[k < 0 ? 0 : 1];
    ctx.save();
    ctx.translate(k * 4.9 * u, top + 3.5 * u);
    ctx.rotate(k < 0 ? a : -a);
    rrect(ctx, k < 0 ? -2.2 * u : 0, -0.5 * u, 2.2 * u, u, 0.2 * u);
    ctx.fillStyle = fill; ctx.fill(); stroke();
    ctx.restore();
  }
  // legs: standing under the body; sitting, the feet point at us below the front edge
  ctx.fillStyle = dk;
  for (const lx of [-4, -2, 1, 3]) {
    if (pose === 'stand') { rrect(ctx, lx * u, -2.4 * u, u, 2.4 * u, 0.15 * u); ctx.fill(); stroke(); }
    else { rrect(ctx, (lx - 0.1) * u, bot - 0.5 * u, 1.2 * u, 1.1 * u, 0.3 * u); ctx.fill(); stroke(); }
  }
  // body
  const body = () => {
    if (!shoulder) { rrect(ctx, -5 * u, top, 10 * u, h * u, 0.3 * u); return; }
    const k = shoulder.k * u, q = shoulder.q * u, a = shoulder.at * u, c = 0.3 * u;
    const L = -5 * u, R = 5 * u, T = top, B = top + h * u;
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.moveTo(L + k, T); ctx.lineTo(R - k, T);
    ctx.quadraticCurveTo(R, T, R, T + k);
    ctx.bezierCurveTo(R, T + k + a * 0.45, R + q, T + k + a * 0.55, R + q, T + k + a);
    ctx.lineTo(R + q, B - c); ctx.quadraticCurveTo(R + q, B, R + q - c, B);
    ctx.lineTo(L - q + c, B); ctx.quadraticCurveTo(L - q, B, L - q, B - c);
    ctx.lineTo(L - q, T + k + a);
    ctx.bezierCurveTo(L - q, T + k + a * 0.55, L, T + k + a * 0.45, L, T + k);
    ctx.quadraticCurveTo(L, T, L + k, T);
    ctx.closePath();
  };
  body();
  ctx.fillStyle = fill; ctx.fill();
  if (clay) {
    ctx.save(); body(); ctx.clip();
    ctx.fillStyle = rgba(dk, 0.5); ctx.fillRect(-5 * u, top + (h === 6 ? 4.2 : h - 1.8) * u, 10 * u, 2 * u);
    ctx.fillStyle = rgba(mix(clay, '#ffffff', 0.35), 0.35);
    ctx.beginPath(); ctx.ellipse(-1.6 * u, top + 1.6 * u, 3.4 * u, 1.3 * u, -0.08, 0, TAU); ctx.fill();
    ctx.restore();
    body(); stroke();
  }
  if (rim) {
    ctx.strokeStyle = rim; ctx.lineWidth = u * 0.3; ctx.lineCap = 'round';
    ctx.beginPath(); ctx.moveTo(-4.6 * u, top + 0.1 * u); ctx.lineTo(4.7 * u, top + 0.1 * u); ctx.lineTo(4.95 * u, top + 2.5 * u); ctx.stroke();
  }
  // face: eyes at (+-2.5u, 2u below the top), mouth 3.7u below the top
  const ec = eyeColor || ink;
  if (eyes) {
    for (const k of [-1, 1]) {
      const ex = k * 2.5 * u, ey = top + 2 * u;
      ctx.fillStyle = ec; ctx.strokeStyle = ec; ctx.lineCap = 'round';
      if (eyes === 'closed') {
        ctx.lineWidth = u * 0.32;
        ctx.beginPath(); ctx.moveTo(ex - 0.8 * u, ey + 0.2 * u); ctx.quadraticCurveTo(ex, ey + 0.8 * u, ex + 0.8 * u, ey + 0.2 * u); ctx.stroke();
      } else if (eyes === 'tired') {
        rrect(ctx, ex - 0.5 * u, ey + 0.05 * u, u, 0.95 * u, 0.15 * u); ctx.fill();
        ctx.lineWidth = u * 0.24;
        ctx.beginPath(); ctx.moveTo(ex - 0.85 * u, ey); ctx.lineTo(ex + 0.85 * u, ey + k * 0.12 * u); ctx.stroke();
      } else if (shine || look) {
        // open, with highlights: the eye and its shine follow the look
        const lx = (look ? look[0] : 0) * 0.22 * u, ly = (look ? look[1] : 0) * 0.3 * u;
        const x0 = ex - 0.5 * u + lx, y0 = ey - u + ly;
        rrect(ctx, x0, y0, u, 2 * u, 0.15 * u); ctx.fill();
        if (shine) {
          ctx.fillStyle = shine;
          // the shine follows the look (looking down: the big one sinks toward the middle)
          const sx = (look ? look[0] : 0) * 0.14 * u, sy = (look ? look[1] : 0) * 0.42 * u;
          ctx.beginPath(); ctx.arc(x0 + 0.34 * u + sx, y0 + 0.52 * u + sy, 0.25 * u, 0, TAU); ctx.fill();
          ctx.beginPath(); ctx.arc(x0 + 0.68 * u + sx * 0.5, y0 + 1.42 * u + sy * 0.3, 0.1 * u, 0, TAU); ctx.fill();
        }
      } else {
        rrect(ctx, ex - 0.5 * u, ey - u, u, 2 * u, 0.15 * u); ctx.fill();
      }
    }
  }
  if (mouth === 'o') {
    const o = 0.75 + 0.25 * breath;
    ctx.fillStyle = clay ? '#4a1f2a' : rgba('#000000', 0.55);
    ctx.beginPath(); ctx.ellipse(0, top + 3.9 * u, 0.6 * u, 0.85 * u * o, 0, 0, TAU); ctx.fill();
    if (lw) { ctx.lineWidth = lw * 0.8; ctx.strokeStyle = ink; ctx.stroke(); }
  } else if (mouth === 'line') {
    ctx.strokeStyle = ec; ctx.lineWidth = u * 0.25;
    ctx.beginPath(); ctx.moveTo(-0.7 * u, top + 3.8 * u); ctx.lineTo(0.7 * u, top + 3.8 * u); ctx.stroke();
  }
  ctx.restore();
}

 * feet, u = Clawd's unit (the body is 10u wide, 6u tall). eyes: 'tired' | 'closed' | null.
 */
export function parent(ctx, { x, y, u = 30, pose = 'stand', color = '#14151b', clay = null, clayDk = null, ink = '#0d0d12', rim = null, eyes = null, eyeColor = '#c9ccd1', lean = 0, mouth = null }) {

}

// Intro (and, frame-exactly, the outro): the phone wakes at 1:59am, the baby's face too
// close, pulls back, waves; the record dot starts with the beat.
import { drawBaby } from '../baby.js';
import { wall, cribBack, fillAll } from '../props.js';
import { SCREEN } from '../tod.js';
import { babyColors } from '../baby.js';
import { clamp, ease, seg, rgba, SELFIE, flashFalloff, clipBeat, CW, CH } from './common.js';
import { lerp } from '../../../../shared/renderer/core/util.js';

export default {
  camera(C) { return { shake: 1.2 }; },
  draw(ctx, C) {
    const { G, lt } = C;
    const words = C.words || [];

    g.addColorStop(0, rgba('#a9c2ff', 0.28));
    g.addColorStop(1, rgba('#a9c2ff', 0));

    if (wake < 1) { ctx.fillStyle = rgba('#000000', 1 - ease.outCubic(wake)); ctx.fillRect(-900, -900, CW + 1800, CH + 1800); }
  },

// Shared bits for clip scenes.
import { clamp, ease, seg, TAU } from '../../../../shared/renderer/core/util.js';
import { rgba } from '../../../../shared/renderer/core/theme.js';
import { FLASH, grade } from '../tod.js';
import { babyColors } from '../baby.js';
import { CW, CH } from '../props.js';

export { clamp, ease, seg, TAU, rgba, CW, CH };

/** Standard selfie head placement (camera space). */
export const SELFIE = { x: 540, y: 1190, r: 300 };

/** Baby colours for this clip: the flash palette at night, the graded room light by day. */
export function babyLight(C, outfit, mode = null) {
  const m = mode || (C.G.light.night > 0.5 ? 'flash' : 'ambient');
  if (m === 'flash') return babyColors(FLASH, outfit);
  // daylight: the baby takes about half the room's colour cast
  return babyColors(C.G.light.night > 0.5 ? C.G : grade(C.G.light.min, undefined, 0.5), outfit);
}

/** Night flash: everything away from the face falls off into the dark. */
export function flashFalloff(ctx, x, y, r, strength = 0.85) {
  const g = ctx.createRadialGradient(x, y, r * 1.05, x, y, r * 3.4);
  g.addColorStop(0, 'rgba(2,3,10,0)');
  g.addColorStop(1, `rgba(2,3,10,${strength})`);
  ctx.fillStyle = g;
  ctx.fillRect(-900, -900, CW + 1800, CH + 1800);
}

/** A quick white pop when the flash fires on a cut. */
export function flashPop(ctx, lt, a = 0.55) {
  if (lt < 0) return;
  const k = Math.exp(-lt / 0.07) * a;
  if (k < 0.01) return;
  ctx.fillStyle = rgba('#ffffff', k);
  ctx.fillRect(-900, -900, CW + 1800, CH + 1800);
}

/** Word start time inside the clip's caption words (by text), or a fallback. */
export function wordT(C, match, fallback = C.start, nth = 0) {
  const m = String(match).toLowerCase();
  let n = nth;
  for (const w of C.words || []) {
    const s = String(w.w).toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9:']/g, '');
    if (s === m || s.includes(m)) { if (n-- === 0) return w.start; }
  }
  return fallback;
}

/** Index of the beat (within the clip) and its phase. */
export function clipBeat(C) {
  const b0 = C.B.beatIndex(C.start), b = C.B.beatIndex(C.t);
  return { i: Math.floor(b - b0 + 1e-6), phase: (b - b0) - Math.floor(b - b0 + 1e-6) };
}
// Final chorus: the day as a filling grid of its clips, 3 wide (a camera-roll grid). Each
// tile lights on its line and plays once, then holds as a thumbnail; the newest tile has a
// white border. The 12th slot is the record button (tomorrow). The 1am tile goes black.
import { ruleFor } from '../clips.js';
import { grade } from '../tod.js';
import { drawCaption } from '../../../../shared/renderer/core/caption.js';
import { rrect } from '../../../../shared/renderer/core/text.js';
import { clamp, ease, seg, rgba, CW, CH } from './common.js';

export const GRID = { cols: 3, rows: 4, gap: 8, top: 124 };
const TW = (CW - (GRID.cols + 1) * GRID.gap) / GRID.cols;
const TH = (CH - GRID.top - (GRID.rows + 1) * GRID.gap) / GRID.rows;
const TILE_SCALE = TW / CW;
const CROP_H = TH / TILE_SCALE;           // camera-space height a tile shows
const CROP_Y = 1190 - CROP_H * 0.56;      // keep the face in the tile

export function tileRect(i) {
  const c = i % GRID.cols, r = Math.floor(i / GRID.cols);
  return { x: GRID.gap + c * (TW + GRID.gap), y: GRID.top + GRID.gap + r * (TH + GRID.gap), w: TW, h: TH };
}

export default {
  camera() { return { shake: 0, kick: 0.8 }; },
  caption() { return false; },
  draw(ctx, C) {
    const { B, env } = C;
    ctx.fillStyle = '#050507';
    ctx.fillRect(-900, -900, CW + 1800, CH + 1800);
    const list = C.params.clauses.map((i) => C.story.clauses[i]);
    const cuts = list.map((c) => B.snap(c.start, 'downbeat'));
    let active = -1;
    cuts.forEach((ct, i) => { if (C.t >= ct - 1e-6) active = i; });
    const endFade = seg(C.t, C.end - B.barDur * 0.5, C.end);
    for (let i = 0; i < GRID.cols * GRID.rows; i++) {
      const R = tileRect(i);
      ctx.save();
      rrect(ctx, R.x, R.y, R.w, R.h, 18);
      ctx.clip();
      if (i < list.length && i <= active) {
        const c = list[i];
        const rule = ruleFor(c);
        const start = cuts[i], dur = (i + 1 < cuts.length ? cuts[i + 1] : C.end) - start;
        // the newest tile plays, older ones hold their thumbnail frame
        const tt = i === active ? C.t : start + dur * 0.8;
        const Ct = {
          ...C, type: rule.type, params: { ...rule.params, clause: c.index }, t: tt, lt: tt - start, start, end: start + dur, dur,
          min: c.min, G: grade(c.min), words: c.words, clause: c, tile: true,
        };
        ctx.translate(R.x, R.y);
        const pop = i === active ? 1 + 0.08 * (1 - ease.outCubic(seg(C.t, start, start + 0.18))) : 1;
        ctx.translate(R.w / 2, R.h / 2); ctx.scale(pop, pop); ctx.translate(-R.w / 2, -R.h / 2);
        ctx.scale(TILE_SCALE, TILE_SCALE);
        ctx.translate(0, -CROP_Y);
        env.drawCameraFrame(ctx, Ct, { chrome: false });
        drawCaption(ctx, tt, c.words, { x: 540, y: CROP_Y + 230, size: 112, minSize: 80, maxW: 1000, family: '"Inter", sans-serif', weight: 900, reveal: i === active ? 'word' : 'all' });
      } else if (i === GRID.cols * GRID.rows - 1) {
        // the 12th slot: the record button, waiting for tomorrow
        ctx.fillStyle = '#111116';
        ctx.fillRect(R.x, R.y, R.w, R.h);
        const pulse = 0.6 + 0.4 * Math.abs(Math.sin(C.t * Math.PI));
        ctx.strokeStyle = rgba('#ffffff', 0.85);
        ctx.lineWidth = 8;
        ctx.beginPath(); ctx.arc(R.x + R.w / 2, R.y + R.h / 2, 62, 0, Math.PI * 2); ctx.stroke();
        ctx.fillStyle = rgba('#ff2d2d', pulse);
        ctx.beginPath(); ctx.arc(R.x + R.w / 2, R.y + R.h / 2, 48, 0, Math.PI * 2); ctx.fill();
      } else {
        ctx.fillStyle = '#0f0f13';
        ctx.fillRect(R.x, R.y, R.w, R.h);
      }
      ctx.restore();
      if (i === active) {
        ctx.strokeStyle = '#ffffff';
        ctx.lineWidth = 7;
        rrect(ctx, R.x - 2, R.y - 2, R.w + 4, R.h + 4, 20);
        ctx.stroke();
      }
    }
    if (endFade > 0) { ctx.fillStyle = rgba('#000000', 0.85 * endFade); ctx.fillRect(-900, -900, CW + 1800, CH + 1800); }
  },
};
// Time of day -> light. Background colour temperature and brightness follow the story
// clock: deep blue night, grey-blue 5am, warm 7am, white noon, gold 6pm, blue again 8pm.
// grade(min) returns a palette of the scene's base colours lit for that minute (cached:
// it depends only on the minute, so it is the same on every worker and frame order).

import { clamp, lerp } from '../../../shared/renderer/core/util.js';
import { rgbOf, rgb, mix } from '../../../shared/renderer/core/theme.js';

// [minute, multiplier rgb, lift rgb]
const KEYS = [
  [0,    [0.11, 0.14, 0.30], [4, 6, 16]],
  [270,  [0.11, 0.14, 0.30], [4, 6, 16]],
  [300,  [0.34, 0.39, 0.52], [10, 12, 20]],   // 5am grey-blue
  [345,  [0.55, 0.58, 0.68], [8, 9, 14]],
  [420,  [1.02, 0.88, 0.72], [10, 6, 0]],     // 7am warm
  [540,  [1.03, 0.99, 0.93], [6, 5, 2]],
  [720,  [1.06, 1.06, 1.04], [8, 8, 8]],      // noon white
  [900,  [1.04, 0.98, 0.88], [6, 4, 0]],
  [1080, [1.04, 0.78, 0.46], [16, 6, 0]],     // 6pm gold
  [1140, [0.96, 0.80, 0.64], [8, 4, 0]],      // 7pm lamplight (bath)
  [1170, [0.70, 0.60, 0.56], [4, 3, 4]],      // 7:30 reading, dim lamp
  [1185, [0.22, 0.26, 0.48], [4, 6, 14]],     // 7:45 lights out
  [1200, [0.24, 0.28, 0.52], [4, 6, 16]],     // 8pm blue
  [1320, [0.12, 0.15, 0.31], [4, 6, 16]],
  [1440, [0.11, 0.14, 0.30], [4, 6, 16]],
];

export function light(min) {
  const m = ((min % 1440) + 1440) % 1440;
  let i = 0;
  while (i < KEYS.length - 2 && KEYS[i + 1][0] <= m) i++;
  const [a, ma, la] = KEYS[i], [b, mb, lb] = KEYS[i + 1];
  const k = clamp((m - a) / (b - a));
  const mul = [0, 1, 2].map((j) => lerp(ma[j], mb[j], k));
  const lift = [0, 1, 2].map((j) => lerp(la[j], lb[j], k));
  const lum = 0.3 * mul[0] + 0.55 * mul[1] + 0.15 * mul[2];
  return { min: m, mul, lift, lum, night: clamp((0.45 - lum) / 0.3), warm: clamp((mul[0] - mul[2]) * 2) };
}

/** Base (true) colours of the world, before lighting. */
export const BASE = {
  wall: '#d9e4dd', wall2: '#c4d3ca', trim: '#f6f3ee', floor: '#b88d63', rug: '#cfd8d2',
  wood: '#d8a878', woodDark: '#a8774f', crib: '#f1ece4', cribShade: '#cfc5b8', sheet: '#dfe8ef', sheetShade: '#b9c7d3',
  // baby Clawd: terracotta clay, a darker clay for the legs, dark ink outline and eyes
  skin: '#d77757', skinShade: '#b35a3d', skinLight: '#f5b394', hair: '#d77757', ink: '#241715', line: '#2b1c19', mouth: '#4a1f2a', white: '#fff8f0',
  blush: '#f0848a', tear: '#8fd0f6', paci: '#76c6b8', paciRing: '#fbf3e4',
  onesie: '#f7f4ee', onesieShade: '#d9d3c8', sack: '#8fa6c6', sackShade: '#6f86a8', towel: '#ffffff', stain: '#e6d3a4',
  window: '#cfe6ff', sky: '#9ccbf2', curtain: '#e8b9a6', fan: '#f4f1ea', fanShade: '#c9c2b6', metal: '#9a9ea4',
  tray: '#f3f0ea', chair: '#efe4d4', chairShade: '#cdbfa9', tile: '#cfe3e6', tileLine: '#a9c4c8', water: '#bfe6f2',
  bottle: '#f4f8fb', milk: '#fbf8ef', nipple: '#e6c28a', book: '#f2a541', book2: '#4fa3c7', food: '#f0b84a', food2: '#9fcf6a',
  parent: '#3c3f4c', parentSkin: '#c8704f', parentShade: '#a5523a', door: '#efe6d8', doorDark: '#2a2622', alarm: '#2b2b30', lamp: '#ffd9a0',
  plush: '#c9a27a', seat: '#5d6b7a', seatShade: '#465260',
};

const cache = new Map();
/** Lit palette for a minute. soft < 1 pulls the light toward neutral (used for the baby,
 *  so faces stay readable while the room carries the colour temperature). */
export function grade(min, base = BASE, soft = 1) {
  const key = Math.floor(((min % 1440) + 1440) % 1440);
  const ck = soft === 1 && base === BASE ? key : `${key}:${soft}:${base === BASE ? 'b' : 'x'}`;
  let G = cache.get(ck);
  if (G) return G;
  const L0 = light(key);
  const L = soft === 1 ? L0 : { ...L0, mul: L0.mul.map((m) => 1 + (m - 1) * soft), lift: L0.lift.map((l) => l * soft) };
  G = { light: L };
  for (const [k, c] of Object.entries(base)) {
    const [r, g, b] = rgbOf(c);
    G[k] = rgb(Math.min(255, r * L.mul[0] + L.lift[0]), Math.min(255, g * L.mul[1] + L.lift[1]), Math.min(255, b * L.mul[2] + L.lift[2]));
  }
  // the face's ink stays legible even at night
  G.ink = mix(G.ink, base.ink, 0.6);
  G.light = L0;
  cache.set(ck, G);
  return G;
}

/** Colours under the phone's flash at night: the face blown out, nothing else lit. */
export const FLASH = (() => {
  const F = {};
  for (const [k, c] of Object.entries(BASE)) F[k] = mix(c, '#ffffff', 0.18);
  F.skin = '#ec9272'; F.skinShade = '#c66a4c'; F.skinLight = '#ffc7aa'; F.ink = '#1b1210'; F.line = '#2a1a16'; F.mouth = '#4a1a22';
  F.blush = '#ff8e94'; F.white = '#ffffff';
  F.sack = '#b9cbe6'; F.sackShade = '#8ea4c6'; F.onesie = '#ffffff'; F.onesieShade = '#dfe3ea';
  return F;
})();

/** Phone-screen glow (intro at 1:59am): cold blue-white light from below. */
export const SCREEN = (() => {
  const F = {};
  for (const [k, c] of Object.entries(BASE)) F[k] = mix(c, '#9fb8ff', 0.35);
  F.skin = '#c28e98'; F.skinShade = '#956b7e'; F.skinLight = '#dcb6c4'; F.ink = '#151a2a'; F.line = '#252338'; F.mouth = '#3a2a4a';
  F.blush = '#d893b4';
  return F;
})();

/** The 16:9 backdrop's sun / moon position for a minute (0..1 across the arc, and which). */
export function skyBody(min) {
  const m = ((min % 1440) + 1440) % 1440;
  if (m >= 360 && m < 1140) return { body: 'sun', p: (m - 360) / 780 };
  const n = m >= 1140 ? m - 1140 : m + 300;
  return { body: 'moon', p: n / 660 };
}
# Replicate (n = 1)

Dense fact-rap from the point of view of the baby being studied. The spine is Netflix's
*Babies* (2020): the title card ("15 babies, filmed over one year, 36 world-renowned
scientists") is the chorus, each verse is a tour of the labs in episode order, and the
bridge is the replication crisis. The hook "Will it replicate?" means three things: cells
dividing, the study being reproduced, and the baby daring the scientists. The turn at the
end: the only result that always replicates is the baby.

Every fact is on `FACTS.md` with a source. Verses are 12 lines at ~96 BPM, one line per bar,
musical-theatre hip-hop patter. "this girl" in verse 3 is the narrator; swap for "this kid".

---

**[Intro]** *(spoken, documentary narrator, over a fetal heartbeat; then the beat)*
> Fifteen babies. Filmed over one year. Thirty-six world-renowned scientists.
> And me. I'm the one in the little cap.

**[Verse 1: Love]**
> Day one: skin to skin, and the oxytocin's climbing.
> Feldman swabs the spit of eighty couples, and the timing's
> identical in dads. "Fatherhood is biological."
> Forty-eight gay fathers, amygdala lit: that's data, not anecdotal.
> Tronick, '68: "This emotion stuff is not what you want to do."
> So he froze a mother's face for two minutes and watched me come unglued:
> I smile, I bid, I fuss, I cry, I chew my hands to cope.
> Then she comes back. We repair. That's the study. That's the hope.

**[Chorus]**
> I'm the subject. I'm the sample. I'm the n of one.
> Put me in the scanner, put me on the slope. I'm not done.
> Cap on my head, swab in my cheek, measure me at seven, then at ten.
> Will it replicate? *(Will it replicate?)*
> Will it replicate? *(Will it replicate?)*
> Fifteen babies. Thirty-six scientists. One year.
> Will it replicate? Ask me in a year.

**[Verse 2: Crawling]**
> Barbu-Roth did nuclear physics; now she's hanging me in air.
> Dots stream under me. I swim. "A newborn's an astronaut," I swear.
> "We're still quadrupeds inside." She strapped a skateboard to my chest:
> two days old, I crossed the mattress. Twice. Three times. Then I had a rest.
> Lampl's neighbor's kid: sixty-two point seven, then sixty-four point five.
> "I'm terrible at this," she thought. Nope. That's just how we arrive:
> up to two and a half centimeters overnight, then sixty-three days of flat.
> Ninety-five percent of babyhood is growth-free. Front page. A big name mocked her for that.
> Adolph raised the slope six degrees a go, up to a sheer drop.
> Grant approached it like the shallow ones and went straight off the top.
> Two thousand three hundred sixty-eight steps an hour, seventeen falls, no stall.
> "Crawling isn't mandatory." Bum-shuffle, bear-crawl, whatever gets you down the hall.

**[Verse 3: Words, Sleep]**
> Hirsh-Pasek splits up Cinderella, pauses in the middle of a clause.
> I turn to where it breathes right. Can't talk yet. I know the laws.
> Saffran: pabeecoo, golatoo, two minutes, forty-five reps each.
> Probability one inside a word, point three across: I'm carving speech.
> Jarvis danced six hours a day, then counted who can learn a sound in the world:
> three birds, five mammals. Parrots, bats, elephants, seals, dolphins, songbirds, hummingbirds. This girl.
> Fenton's app: a thousand babies, six million events, logged to the minute:
> one-point-five million sleeps, two-point-five million feeds, the diapers, a million and a half in it.
> Random at one month, a hint by eight, a rhythm by a year. *(He hasn't published it.)*
> Blumberg says my twitch is sonar: one ping, a thousand neurons, mapping where my limbs fit.
> Spencer: hippocampus small, so I file in bouts; the spindles move the day
> from hippocampus to cortex. Two naps and a night. That's not lazy. That's the way.

**[Chorus]**

**[Verse 4: First Steps, then Part Two]** *(double-time on the Part Two lines)*
> Dominici wires my legs at two days old: two muscle patterns, and I step;
> toddlers add two more. Rats, cats, macaques, guineafowl: same ancient rep.
> Cowgill hauled a hundred thirty pounds through fifteen museums, six hundred kids' bones in the stack.
> Reviewer 2 said "children's bones don't respond to exercise." Toddler femurs are wide ovals. Take that back.
> Walle: walkers know more words than crawlers, and they hear the exact same amount.
> It's you: "Du—" "Duck." "Duck!" Three hundred seventy-four words in five minutes. I count.
> Part Two: Hespos hangs a block in midair; I stare twice as long. I know how things fall.
> Kang Lee: six months, I tell two monkeys apart. Nine months, like you, not at all.
> Saxe scans her own son, awake: face patch, place patch, same spots as yours.
> Hamlin's puppet climbs; I reach for the helper. *(Hold that thought. It's back in the bridge, of course.)*
> Carpenter: twelve months, I point to show, not to get. You look. That's the human part.
> Warneken "drops" a cup; eighteen months, I hand it back in ten seconds. Pay me with a toy and I'll stop. Also the human part.

**[Bridge: the part you skip]** *(half-time, sparse, the baby as Reviewer 2)*
> Now the part you skip.
> The Wonder Weeks: ten leaps on a calendar. Tested on four babies by a grad student. Nothing. The man behind it lost his job in '98.
> Mozart effect: thirty-six college students, ten minutes, never once a baby. Georgia spent a hundred and five grand on CDs for newborns.
> Baby Einstein: a month of DVDs, zero new words. Disney refunded fifteen ninety-nine a disc.
> Neonatal imitation: six babies in '77 stuck their tongues out. A hundred and six in 2016 did nothing.
> The helper and the hinderer: *Nature*, 2007. ManyBabies 4: five hundred sixty-seven babies, thirty-seven labs. Forty-nine point three percent. A coin.
> Thirty million words: forty-two families, one hour a month.
> So when you ask me... will it replicate?
> *(beat drops out)*
> Here's what did.
> Me. Brain volume up a hundred and one percent in year one. Twenty-five hundred synapses a neuron, then more, then pruned. Weight tripled. Length up half.
> I'm the replication. I'm the only result that always comes in.

**[Final Chorus]**
> I'm the subject. I'm the sample. I'm the n of one.
> Put me in the scanner, put me on the slope. I'm not done.
> Cap on my head, swab in my cheek, measure me at seven, then at ten.
> Will it replicate? *(I did.)*
> Will it replicate? *(I did.)*
> Fifteen babies. Thirty-six scientists. One year.
> Will it replicate? Ask me in a year.

**[Outro]** *(spoken; the beat stops; heartbeat)*
> "Hardest problem in the universe. We should let them sleep."
> n equals one.

# Suno run pack: Replicate (n = 1)

Lyrics files: `suno_lyrics_v1.txt` is the full song (about 6,100 characters), which is over
Suno's 5,000-character custom-mode limit. `suno_lyrics_v1_short.txt` is the run file (under
5,000): same song minus the Dominici and Walle couplets, a four-line second chorus, and
shorter section tags. Use the short file for every run; restore the cut couplets only if a
future model raises the limit.

## Run A: musical-theatre hip-hop patter (primary)

```
~/.local/bin/suno generate \
  --title "Replicate (n = 1)" \
  --model v5.5 \
  --vocal female \
  --tags "musical theatre hip-hop, 96 bpm, boom-bap drums, rapid patter rap with crisp diction, brass and string stabs on the chorus, gang vocal echoes, upright bass, piano, storytelling, witty, documentary spoken intro and outro, half-time sparse bridge, triumphant final chorus" \
  --exclude "mumble rap, autotune, trap hi-hats, EDM drop, slow ballad, metal, lo-fi, slurred vocals" \
  --style-influence 75 --weirdness 30 \
  --lyrics-file /Users/jalex/projects/clawdpop/songs/baby-facts/suno_lyrics_v1_short.txt \
  --wait --download /Users/jalex/projects/clawdpop/songs/baby-facts/audio/runA
```

## Run B: the clawdpop house sound (keeps the two videos in one family)

```
~/.local/bin/suno generate \
  --title "Replicate (n = 1) [club]" \
  --model v5.5 \
  --vocal female \
  --tags "eurodance, 128 bpm, four-on-the-floor, rapid half-time rap verses with crisp diction, sidechained supersaw chorus, vocoder echoes on 'will it replicate', brass stabs, big drop after the bridge, spoken intro and outro" \
  --exclude "mumble, slurred vocals, slow ballad, acoustic, metal, lo-fi" \
  --style-influence 70 --weirdness 35 \
  --lyrics-file /Users/jalex/projects/clawdpop/songs/baby-facts/suno_lyrics_v1_short.txt \
  --wait --download /Users/jalex/projects/clawdpop/songs/baby-facts/audio/runB
```

## What to listen for

- Numbers must be intelligible: "two thousand three hundred sixty-eight", "forty-nine point
  three percent", "sixty-two point seven". If Suno garbles them, spell numbers as digits in the
  lyrics file for the next run.
- The beat must actually drop out for "Here's what did." and return on "Me."
- The Part Two lines should be double-time, not rushed into unintelligibility; if they are,
  move them to their own `[Verse 5]` block at normal speed.

## Run C / C2: radio cut (2026-10-18)

Lyrics: `suno_lyrics_v2_radio.txt`, the short sheet minus seven lines (Lampl's "terrible at
this" and "ninety-five percent" lines, Grant, both Hirsh-Pasek lines, Fenton's event counts,
neonatal imitation, thirty million words). Aim: a take nearer 3:30 than 4:29 for the video.
C uses run A's tags and settings; C2 adds "tight arrangement, no instrumental breaks".

import json
A=json.load(open('songs/baby-facts/audio/runA/data/audio.json'))
r=A['loudness']['rms_db']; hop=A['loudness']['hop']
def peaks(a,b):
    out=[]
    i0=int(a/hop); i1=min(len(r)-1,int(b/hop))
    for i in range(i0+3,i1-3):
        if r[i]==max(r[i-12:i+13]) and r[i]-min(r[i-25:i+1])>6: out.append((round(i*hop,2), r[i]))
    return out
print('intro', peaks(0,16))

import numpy as np
x=np.fromfile('$S/low.f32',dtype=np.float32); sr=4000
hop=40
e=np.sqrt(np.convolve(x**2, np.ones(hop*2)/(hop*2), 'same'))[::hop]
db=20*np.log10(e+1e-9)
t=np.arange(len(db))*hop/sr
def pk(a,b):
    out=[]
    for i in range(int(a*100),int(b*100)):
        if i<30 or i>len(db)-30: continue
        if db[i]==db[i-20:i+21].max() and db[i]-db[i-30:i].min()>8: out.append(round(t[i],2))
    return out
p=pk(0,16); print('intro', p); print(np.diff(p).round(3))
q=pk(258,269.6); print('outro', q); print(np.diff(q).round(3))
for a in list(range(0,6))+list(range(264,270)): print(a, ' '.join(f'{v:.0f}' for v in db[a*100:(a+1)*100:4]))

1:# Netflix *Babies* (2020) and the infant-science literature: fact sheet for a dense song
9:## 0. Series facts
15:- Part 1 sat in Netflix's Top 10 for several weeks (Northwestern release); WSU says it "rose to number seven." https://news.northwestern.edu/stories/2020/02/netflix-babies-docu-series-features-northwestern-psychology-professor-susan-hespos **[solid]**
16:- Opening narration (Ep 1), Andre Fenton: "As a scientist and a new parent, I realize that babies are born totally helpless. But they're going to grow up to be masters of the universe. How does that happen?" **[solid, transcript]**
27:- The microbiome scientist is Susan Lynch (UCSF), not Dominguez-Bello; the language scientists are Hirsh-Pasek, Saffran and Jarvis, not Kuhl. **[solid]**
31:## 1. Episode by episode
33:### Ep 1 "Love" (Part 1)
38:- Next: "80 couples," saliva sampled in the first months; "Mothers' and fathers' level of oxytocin were identical." Gordon, Zagoory-Sharon, Leckman and Feldman 2010, *Biological Psychiatry* 68:377 (160 first-time parents at 6 weeks and 6 months). https://sc
43:- The paper: Abraham et al. 2014, *PNAS* 111:9792 — 89 parents (20 primary-care mothers, 21 secondary-care fathers, 48 primary-care gay fathers); gay fathers showed mother-like amygdala plus father-like superior-temporal-sulcus activity; amygdala–STS connec
45:- **Ed Tronick** (UMass Boston; Harvard Medical School). Arrived at Harvard psychology in 1968, when "everyone was doing hard science"; a professor put an arm round him: "This emotion stuff is not what you want to be doing." **[solid, transcript]**
46:- Still-face: mothers of 3-, 4- and 5-month-olds "stop reacting to the baby for about two minutes"; babies smile, bid, eventually cry, then bring hands to mouth to self-comfort. Tronick, Als, Adamson, Wise and Brazelton 1978, *J Am Acad Child Psychiatry* 17
48:- Tronick's parenting line: "Trust your baby to tell you what it is that he or she needs and trust your own instincts... That's how the two of you fall in love with one another." **[solid, transcript]**
53:### Ep 2 "First Food"
57:- Dairy cows favor daughters: 2.39 million lactation records from 1.49 million Holsteins show ~1.3% more milk (~445 kg over two lactations) after a heifer calf. Hinde, Carpenter, Clay and Bradford 2014, *PLoS ONE* 9:e86169. https://journals.plos.org/plosone
70:### Ep 3 "Crawling"
73:- **Marianne Barbu-Roth** (CNRS / Université de Paris). "I started to be a nuclear physicist... studying the movement of electrons and atoms"; a newborn is "an astronaut coming back from the moon... now stuck with the gravity." **[solid, transcript]**
75:- Optic flow: moving black dots projected beneath a suspended newborn make it move "literally swimming. Swimming in the air." Barbu-Roth et al. 2009, *Child Development* 80:8 — 48 three-day-olds; more air-steps to forward-translating flow than to rotating o
77:- The "Crawlerskate" (published as the Crawliskate): a mini-skateboard under the trunk; first baby tested 2016 after "one or two years" of building; two-day-olds "even crossed the mattress once or two or three times." Forma et al. 2019, *Child Development*.
79:- **Michelle Lampl** (Emory, Center for the Study of Human Health; MD-PhD anthropologist). 1979: her neighbor's baby measured 62.7 cm one day and 64.5 cm the next, then 64.5 for days: "I'm really terrible at this... that's way too much." **[solid, transcrip
84:- The paper: Lampl, Veldhuis and Johnson 1992, *Science* 258:801 — 31 infants (10 weekly, 18 semiweekly, 3 daily) over 21 months; saltations 0.5–2.5 cm; stasis 2–63 days; "90 to 95 percent of normal development during infancy is growth-free." https://pubmed
86:- Follow-up: length spurts follow sleep bursts within 48 hours; odds of a spurt rise a median 43% per extra sleep episode and 20% per extra hour (23 infants, 5,798 diary days). Lampl and Johnson 2011, *Sleep* 34:641. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PM
87:- Head circumference grows in jumps too, coupled to length (40 infants, 2,616 measurements). Lampl et al. 2011, *Early Hum Dev* 87:361. **[solid]**
90:- **Karen Adolph** (NYU Infant Action Lab). Studied fine art in college; a professor handed her a plant: "Draw this... you need to really look at it and understand how it grows." **[solid, transcript]**
92:- Marlow stopped at the brink; Dax switched to a bear crawl on hands and feet. "The babies who make smart decisions... generate perceptual information by looking and by touching." **[solid, transcript]**
93:- "It takes about 20 weeks of crawling experience before a baby is showing adult-like accuracy when they're looking at an impossibly steep slope." (cf. Adolph 1997, *Monographs SRCD* 62(3).) **[solid]**
97:- Lit: of 28 infants followed from first crawling to walking, 15 belly-crawled first and 13 skipped straight to hands-and-knees; belly-crawlers became better hands-and-knees crawlers. Adolph, Vereijken and Denny 1998, *Child Dev* 69:1299. **[solid]**
101:### Ep 4 "First Words"
104:- **Kathy Hirsh-Pasek** (Temple University Infant Language Lab; Brookings). Was "headed toward being a musician"; her aha moment was a three-year-old at a pool complaining about her brother's ballgame "with the sophisticated grammar and language skills of 
106:- The paper: Hirsh-Pasek et al. 1987, *Cognition* 26:269, "Clauses are perceptual units for young infants" (7–10-month-olds). **[solid]**
111:- **Jenny Saffran** (University of Wisconsin–Madison Infant Learning Lab). Origin story: her mother studied brain damage; a patient lost every noun except "shopping center" — "The shopping center went to the shopping center to buy the shopping center." **[
114:- The paper: Saffran, Aslin and Newport 1996, *Science* 274:1926 — 8-month-olds segment words using transitional probabilities of 1.0 within words vs 0.33 across. https://psych.wisc.edu/news/saffrans-infant-learning-lab-featured-on-netflix-docuseries-babie
118:- **Erich Jarvis** (Rockefeller University; HHMI). "I was once a dancer... I was dancing six hours a day"; at 18 in 1988, at his senior concert, he chose science "and I never turned back." **[solid, transcript]**
119:- Vocal learners across tens of thousands of vertebrate species: "Three birds and five mammals. That's the hummingbirds, the parrots, songbirds, elephants, bats, dolphins, seals, and human." Jarvis 2019, *Science* 366:50. **[solid]**
122:- "In 2012, an intriguing discovery was made that humans have an extra copy of one gene... SRGAP2," which "maintains extra connections in the entire human brain." Dennis et al. 2012, *Cell* 149:912 — duplicated ~3.4, ~2.4 and ~1 million years ago; SRGAP2C 
126:### Ep 5 "Sleep"
129:- **Andre Fenton** (NYU Center for Neural Science). "Getting your baby to sleep through the night turns out to be a universal human challenge." Became a sleep researcher because "all hell broke loose" when he couldn't get his daughter to sleep. **[solid, t
132:- **Pascal Wallisch** (NYU): "about 1,000 babies... six million events made up out of about 1.5 million sleep events, 2.5 million eating events, and 1.5 million diaper change events." **[solid, transcript]**
134:- The year-one pattern: sleep after the morning feed, again about five hours later in early afternoon, and overnight. Fenton: the transition "is something that almost all babies do. And it'll take about a year." **[unverified, unpublished]**
135:- **Mark Blumberg** (University of Iowa). "I've always been a terrible sleeper... we study the things that we're bad at." Never uses an alarm clock. **[solid, transcript]**
142:- "A single twitch can produce activity in hundreds, probably thousands, of neurons... you're mapping your body." Blumberg, Marques and Iida 2013, *Curr Biol* 23:R532. **[solid]**
143:- Human follow-up: in 22 infants, twitches during quiet sleep rise sharply from ~3 months and are time-locked to sleep spindles. Sokoloff et al. 2021, *Curr Biol*. Lab page: "highlighted in Episode 5." https://blumberg.lab.uiowa.edu/node/41 **[solid]**
144:- **Rebecca Spencer** (UMass Amherst SomneuroLab). "I'm a lark"; postdoc at UC Berkeley in the mid-2000s when sleep-and-memory work took off. **[solid, transcript]**
147:- Landmark paper: Kurdziel, Duclos and Spencer 2013, *PNAS* 110:17267 — 40 preschoolers, ~78-minute classroom naps, ~10% more recall after napping, spindle density r = 0.65 with memory gain; only habitual nappers benefit. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/artic
148:- Filmed March 2019 at UMass. https://www.massachusetts.edu/ada-exception-archive/umass-amherst-researcher-rebecca-spencer-featured-netflix-series-and-pbs-nova **[solid]**
149:- Fenton's closer: "It's solving one of the hardest problems in the universe. We should let them sleep." **[solid, transcript]**
152:### Ep 6 "First Steps"
155:- **Nadia Dominici** (Vrije Universiteit Amsterdam; Italian physicist turned neurophysiologist). Newborn stepping: "You support about 70% of the baby's weight and you put its feet on a flat surface... one leg slowly in front of the other." **[solid, transc
157:- Newborn stepping needs only two muscle-activation patterns (weight support and swing); toddlers keep both and add two more (touchdown and push-off). Dominici et al. 2011, *Science* 334:997. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22096202/ **[solid]**
160:- **Libby Cowgill** (University of Missouri; biological anthropologist, powerlifter and CrossFit instructor). "I come from a long line of people who like to pick up very heavy objects and put them down again"; her grandfather did a gymnastics routine on to
166:- The paper: Cowgill, Warrener, Pontzer and Ocobock 2010, *Am J Phys Anthropol* 143:52 — normalized mediolateral forces significantly higher in young walkers than adults. https://anthropology.missouri.edu/news/prof-libby-cowgill-featured-netflix-series-bab
167:- **Eric Walle** (UC Merced Interpersonal Development Lab). As a grad student he handed parents a language survey "primarily just to distract the parents"; the accidental result: "walking infants had significantly larger vocabulary sizes... regardless of t
168:- The paper: Walle and Campos 2014, *Dev Psychol* 50:336 — walking onset brought a jump in receptive and productive vocabulary; replicated in Chinese infants (He, Walle and Campos 2015, *Infancy* 20:283). https://doi.org/10.1037/a0033238 **[solid; parent-r
172:- Caveat: a later Walle follow-up found 13-month crawlers knew as many words as 13-month walkers. **[contested]**
175:### Ep 7 "What Babies Know" (Part 2)
178:- **Susan Hespos** (Northwestern Infant Cognition Lab; now Western Sydney University MARCS). Babies look about twice as long at a block hanging in midair as at a supported one: "They know this stuff way earlier than we thought." **[solid, HuffPost]**
180:- Lit: 5-month-olds expect liquids to pour and solids to tumble from an upturned cup. Hespos, Ferry and Rips 2009, *Psychol Sci* 20:603. **[solid]**
181:- Lit: 7- and 9-month-olds abstract same/different from four exemplars (N = 64); the single-exemplar replication failed (N = 26). Ferry, Hespos and Gentner 2015, *Child Dev* 86:1386. **[solid]**
182:- Wikipedia lists her as "Part 2, Episode 1"; her lab was filmed in Evanston in fall 2019. https://news.northwestern.edu/stories/2020/02/netflix-babies-docu-series-features-northwestern-psychology-professor-susan-hespos **[solid]**
183:- **Kang Lee** (OISE, University of Toronto). Filmed in his lab, on campus and in Toronto neighborhoods on "how babies learn about faces about developing racial biases," with 6- and 9-month-olds. https://www.oise.utoronto.ca/about/news/07-02-20-oise-profes
186:- HuffPost's Part 2 item "babies can tell monkeys apart but adults can't" (3-month-olds distinguish individual monkey faces; by one year it is pruned) matches the classic: 6-month-olds discriminate monkey faces, 9-month-olds and adults do not. Pascalis, de
190:- Lit: Deen et al. 2017, *Nat Commun* 8:13995 — 17 infants scanned, 9 usable, aged 4–6 months; face- and scene-preferring regions already in adult locations. **[solid]**

203:### Ep 9 "Senses"
212:### Ep 10 "Relationships"
218:- Puppet show: **Kiley Hamlin** (UBC Centre for Infant Cognition). Infants watch a character helped or hindered and are offered both; "babies seem to respond in ways that show greater preference for helpers over harmers." https://ubyssey.ca/science/netflix
220:- Lit: Hamlin, Wynn and Bloom 2007, *Nature* 450:557 — 6- and 10-month-olds reach for the helper over the hinderer. https://doi.org/10.1038/nature06288 **[solid paper; exact counts unverified]**
221:- Lit: 3-month-olds by looking time — 10 of 12 preferred helper over hinderer, and 10 of 12 neutral over hinderer, a "negativity bias." Hamlin, Wynn and Bloom 2010, *Dev Sci* 13:923. **[solid, small n]**
222:- Lit: 8-month-olds prefer a puppet that acts badly toward an antisocial puppet; 21-month-olds hand treats to good puppets and take them from bad ones. Hamlin et al. 2011, *PNAS* 108:19931. **[solid]**
223:- Lit: "Not like me = bad" — 200 nine- and 14-month-olds choose graham crackers or green beans, then favor the puppet who harms the opposite-taste puppet. Hamlin et al. 2013, *Psychol Sci*. **[solid]**
225:- ManyBabies 4: 1,018 infants tested, 567 included (5.5–10.5 months), 37 labs on five continents — 49.3% chose the helper. Lucca et al. 2025, *Dev Sci* 28:e13581. **[solid replication; original effect now contested]**
226:- Pointed finger: **Malinda Carpenter** (University of St Andrews, Baby and Child "ABC" Lab). Netflix "came to St Andrews to film the lab"; Wikipedia: "series 2 episode 4 Relationships... featured Carpenter's team's studies." https://developmentlab.wp.st-a
227:- Lit: joint attention, gaze following and pointing emerge between 9 and 15 months in 24 infants followed monthly. Carpenter, Nagell and Tomasello 1998, *Monographs SRCD* 63(4). **[solid]**
228:- Lit: 12-month-olds point declaratively to share attention and interest, and stop pointing when the adult ignores the referent. Liszkowski et al. 2004, *Dev Sci* 7:297. **[solid]**
230:### Ep 11 "Nature and Nurture"
240:### Ep 12 "Toddlers"
243:- **Felix Warneken** (University of Michigan; formerly MPI Leipzig and Harvard). On camera he "clumsily knocks a cup off a table" and a toddler walks over to hand it back. https://michigantoday.umich.edu/2020/07/23/children-show-altruism-at-a-young-age **[
244:- Lit: Warneken and Tomasello 2006, *Science* 311:1301 — 24 eighteen-month-olds helped in 10 situations (out-of-reach objects, cabinets, wrong results); three human-raised chimpanzees helped only with reaching. **[solid; N via Michigan release]**
246:- Rewards backfire: 20-month-olds given a toy for helping later helped less than those praised or given nothing. Warneken and Tomasello 2008, *Dev Psychol* 44:1785. **[solid]**
254:## 2. Literature greatest hits, by theme
256:### Sleep
266:- Naps consolidate preschool memory by ~10%; spindle density predicts the gain (r = 0.65). Kurdziel, Duclos and Spencer 2013, *PNAS*. **[solid]**
268:- Blumberg's informal estimate: an infant's limbs twitch "at least 40,000 times a day." https://now.uiowa.edu/node/37661 **[informal estimate]**
271:### Crying
282:### Feeding
297:### Movement
298:- Learning to walk: 12–19-month-olds average 2,368 steps and 17 falls per hour of free play and travel 701 m — "7.7 American football fields" — per hour. Adolph et al. 2012, *Psychol Sci* 23:1387 (151 infants). https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3591
301:- No transfer across postures: experienced 12-month crawlers never attempt a 90-cm cliff, but 10 of 16 (63%) 12-month novice walkers go over it. Kretch and Adolph 2013, *Child Dev* 84:226. **[solid]**
317:### Language
322:- Statistical learning: 8-month-olds segment words from 2 minutes of monotone nonsense. Saffran, Aslin and Newport 1996, *Science* 274:1926. **[solid]**
325:- The 30-million-word gap: Hart and Risley 1995 recorded 42 families one hour a month for 2.5 years; 2,153 vs 616 words per hour by SES, extrapolated to 45M vs 13M words by age 4. https://www.aft.org/ae/spring2003/hart_risley **[solid as reported]**
326:- Not reproduced: counting all caregivers and overheard speech erases much of the gap (Sperry, Sperry and Miller 2019, *Child Dev* 90:1303); LENA recordings of 329 families (49,765 hours) put the gap nearer 4 million words by age 4 (Gilkerson et al. 2017).
328:- Baby videos: each daily hour of baby DVDs went with 6–8 fewer understood words at 8–16 months. Zimmerman, Christakis and Meltzoff 2007, *J Pediatr* 151:364. **[solid, correlational]**
329:- A month of Baby Einstein taught 12–18-month-olds no new words; parents who liked the DVD overestimated learning. DeLoache et al. 2010, *Psychol Sci* 21:1570. **[solid]**
333:- Pointing emerges ~12 months and predicts later language (meta-analytic r = .52 concurrent; Colonnesi et al. 2010, *Dev Rev*); declarative pointing is the human-specific part (Tomasello, Carpenter and Liszkowski 2007, *Child Dev* 78:705). **[solid; effect
340:### Cognition
350:- Memory: 9-month-olds imitate after 24 hours, 14-month-olds after a week (Meltzoff 1988); adults' earliest memories date to ~3–3.5 years in a corpus of over 11,000 memories (Rubin 2000, *Memory* 8:265). **[solid]**
353:- Neonatal imitation: Meltzoff and Moore 1977 (*Science*, 6 infants aged 12–21 days) vs 106 newborns tested at 1, 3, 6 and 9 weeks with 11 models who imitated nothing. Oostenbroek et al. 2016, *Curr Biol* 26:1334. **[contested, leaning debunked]**
354:- Cross-modal: 1-month-olds looked longer at the pacifier shape they had sucked unseen (Meltzoff and Borton 1979, *Nature* 282:403), later not replicated; shapes bias color preference in 2–3-month-olds but not 8-month-olds (Wagner and Dobkins 2011, *Psycho
357:- Perceptual narrowing for faces: 6-month-olds discriminate individual monkey faces; 9-month-olds and adults cannot. Pascalis, de Haan and Nelson 2002, *Science* 296:1321. **[solid]**
360:### Social
361:- Oxytocin: 80 couples, fathers' levels equal to mothers' (Gordon et al. 2010); gay primary-caregiving fathers' amygdalae respond like mothers' (Abraham et al. 2014, *PNAS*, 89 parents). **[solid]**
365:- The popular "matched only ~30% of the time, mismatches repaired in seconds" is Tronick's own summary of Tronick and Cohn 1989 (54 dyads), not a figure in a primary abstract. **[contested number]**
373:- Helping: 18-month-olds help within seconds, before eye contact (Warneken and Tomasello 2006); a toy reward makes 20-month-olds help less (2008). **[solid]**
377:- Helper/hinderer: ManyBabies 4 (567 infants, 37 labs) found 49.3% helper choice. **[contested]**
385:- Mothers of 11-month-old girls underestimated, mothers of boys overestimated, their babies' crawling on slopes; actual performance was identical. Mondschein, Adolph and Tamis-LeMonda 2000, *J Exp Child Psychol* 77:304. **[solid]**
393:### Senses
395:- Fixed focus: alert newborns focus best at a median 19 cm (~7.5 inches); accommodation "approximates adult performance by the 4th month." Haynes, White and Held 1965, *Science* 148:528. **[solid]**
405:- Head and body: a newborn's head is about a quarter of body length vs about an eighth in adults (textbook); Barbu-Roth's on-air "one third of the weight" is not a standard figure. **[solid / pop]**
407:### Growth
408:- Saltatory growth: bursts of 0.5–2.5 cm separated by 2–63 days of nothing; 90–95% of infancy is growth-free (Lampl, Veldhuis and Johnson 1992, *Science*). **[solid, contested]**
410:- Brain volume +101% in year one and +15% in year two; gray matter +149%, cerebellum +240% in year one; ~83% of adult volume by age 2 (98 children). Knickmeyer et al. 2008, *J Neurosci* 28:12176. **[solid]**
412:- Measured synapses: ~2,500 per neuron at birth, peaking 50–60% above adult density; density peaks at ~3 months in auditory cortex and after 15 months in prefrontal cortex, then is pruned. Huttenlocher and Dabholkar 1997, *J Comp Neurol* 387:167. **[solid]
417:### Myths and busted claims (jokes live here)
418:- **Wonder Weeks**: ten "leaps" on a fixed calendar. Groningen PhD student Carolina de Weerth, with Paul van Geert, tested the schedule in 4 infants and found nothing (1998). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Wonder_Weeks **[pop / contested]**
419:- De Weerth said Frans Plooij pressured her not to publish; the university let Plooij go in January 1998 (Volkskrant, 14 Jan 1998). Later studies: 28 infants, no pattern; 18 infants, fussiness only by maternal report. **[contested]**
420:- **Mozart effect**: 36 college students, a spatial-reasoning bump lasting ~10–15 minutes (Rauscher, Shaw and Ky 1993, *Nature*); never tested on babies; a 39-study meta-analysis found effects three times larger in Rauscher-affiliated labs and "little evid
437:## 3. Hooks: 30 quotable, numeric, surprising lines (each with a source)
439:1. A toddler takes about 2,368 steps and falls 17 times an hour — roughly 14,000 steps and 100 falls a day. Adolph et al. 2012, *Psychol Sci*. **[solid; daily figures extrapolated]**
440:2. Babies grow up to 2.5 cm overnight, then nothing for up to 63 days; 90–95% of infancy is growth-free. Lampl 1992, *Science*. **[solid, contested]**
441:3. On air: 62.7 cm one day, 64.5 the next — Lampl's first thought was "I'm really terrible at this." Netflix *Babies* Ep 3. **[solid]**
